NOTICE OF PUBLIC HEARING AND AGENDA
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
Tuesday, March 8, 2022
ONE OR MORE SUPERVISORS MAY ATTEND VIA VIDEO AND/OR TELEPHONICALLY 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors will hold a meeting open to the public on Tuesday, March 8, 2022, in the Supervisors’
Chamber in the Navajo County Governmental Complex, 100 East Code Talkers Drive, Holbrook, Arizona. The Board may vote to
meet in a closed executive session to discuss certain matters and for legal advice on any item as indicated on the following
agenda, pursuant to A.R.S. § 38-431.03(A) (3). 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors finds that it is in the best interest of the Community to conduct this public meeting using
a hybrid method in order to serve the public interest. Board members will participate in the meeting, in-person in the Board
of Supervisors Chambers in Holbrook, or via Zoom. Limited seating will be available for the public.
To Join Zoom Meeting now,
click here:  https://us06web.zoom.us/j/84264615494?pwd=a3ZVWS9YWUpSV1BOWDRvUVVNbHFyZz09
Web Conference:
Call In Number: 1-346-248-7799 or 1-669-900-6833 (you may use either teleconference number)
Meeting ID: 842 6461 5494
Passcode: 945459
If you would like to speak during Call to the Public please take the following actions: 
1. Email Melissa Buckley at, melissa.buckley@navajocountyaz.gov
2. Identify the Board of Supervisors' meeting date, the agenda item # and title.
3. Name & Telephone Number
4. Comments or questions should be sent by 5:00 pm. the day prior to the Board meeting.
Persons with disabilities who need accommodation to attend or participate in the meeting may contact the Clerk of the Board’s office
at (928) 524-4053; at least 48 hours prior to the meeting, so an accommodation can be arranged.
Items on the agenda may be considered out of order at the Chairman’s discretion. A copy of the agenda background material
provided to the Board, except items to be considered in executive session, is available for public inspection at Navajo County
website, www.navajocountyaz.gov
“NOTICE TO PARENTS AND LEGAL GUARDIANS: Parents and legal guardians have the right to consent before Navajo County makes a video or
voice recording of a minor child, pursuant to A.R.S. § 1-602(A)(9). The Navajo County Board of Supervisors regular meetings are recorded and may
be viewed on Navajo County’s website, and social media pages. If you permit your child to attend/participate in a televised Navajo County Board of
Supervisors meeting, a recording will be made, and your child’s picture may be posted on Navajo County’s social media pages. You may exercise
your right not to consent by not allowing your child to attend/participate in the meeting.” 
9:00 a.m. Call To Order: Invocation and Pledge of Allegiance
           
1. CALL TO THE PUBLIC: Individuals may address the Board on any relevant issue for up to
5 minutes. At the close of the call to the public, Board members may not respond to any
comments but may respond to criticism, ask staff to review a matter or ask that a matter be
placed on a future agenda.
 
 
2. CONSENT AGENDA: These items are considered to be routine or administrative in nature
and will be approved in a single motion. A Board member may request for any item to be
removed from the consent agenda and considered on the regular agenda:
 
 
a. Minutes: February 22, 2022  
 
b. Warrant and Voucher Report over $1000, February 2022  
 
c. Constable Reports: Show Low Precinct #5 and Pinetop-Lakeside Precinct #6

<!-- page 2 -->
d. Sympathy Letters for: Patrick Zinicola (County Attorney), JoAnn DeWitt (Public Defender),
Jacob DeWitt (Public Defender), Melanie Sleeper (Public Works), Tyson Bochinclonny
(Sheriff), Jessica Freeman (Sheriff), Christine Yazzie (Jail Operations) and Salli O'Hair
(Sheriff)
 
 
e. Authorization for Clerk of the Board to request renewal of undercover plates for the Navajo
County Probation Department
 
 
f. Approval of 32 Back Tax Land Parcels sold over the counter in the amount of $16,507.89  
 
g. Appointment of Daniel Thomas Spencer, James Robert Tapscott, Sandra Christine Johnson,
Cara Ann Massa, and Donna Anne Shurwin as Republican Precinct Committeemen for their
respective precincts
 
 
h. Grant application for the Gila River Indian Community to fund the replacement of 2 K9 patrol
vehicles
 
 
i. Request for ARRF Funds by the Holbrook High School Choir in the amount of $600.00  
 
j. Memoranda of Agreement between Northeastern Arizona Local Workforce Development
Board and Stanford Store, Old Concho Community Assistance Center, and
Springerville-Eagar Chamber of Commerce effective through June 30, 2024
 
 
k. Memorandum of Agreement between Northeastern Arizona Local Workforce Development
Board and Apache County Libraries effective through June 30, 2024
 
 
l. Revised Memorandum of Understanding between The Northeastern Arizona Local Workforce
Development Board and the Northeastern Arizona Community Resource Network
 
 
m. Intergovernmental Agreement between Pinal County Local Workforce Development Board
and Navajo County as Chief Elected Official for the Northeastern Arizona Local Workforce
Development Board
 
 
n. Intergovernmental Agreement with the Town of Taylor for their request for financial
assistance for the Papermill Road improvement project in the amount of $50,000
 
 
o. Appointment of Ms. Sadie Lister to the Planning and Zoning Commission representing District
II
 
 
p. Adoption of Resolution Number 05 - 2022 approving a 24-month time extension request to
the previously approved Master Site Plan, also known as Wendover Pines, for up to 450
residential units, a commercial-retail space, and open space on approximately 295 acres
 
 
q. Annual 9-1-1 call handling services renewal and purchase of equipment and services for 9-1-1
call handling through Solacom/Next Gen/Com Tech 
 
 
r. Contracts signed by County Manager/Assistant County Manager pursuant to Board of
Supervisors Resolution: Ironside Engineering & Development for Rocky Arroyo Crossing;
Painted Sky Engineering & Survey for Linden Trails Topo; and Painted Sky Engineering &
Survey for Lee Reese Drive Staking

<!-- page 3 -->
3. BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:  
 
a. Presentation to Zachary Bates for his success in the Coldwater Rumble 100-Mile race.  
Presented By: Chairwoman Dawnafe Whitesinger
 
b. Presentation to Alchesay High School Girls Basketball Team for winning the 3A State
Championship
 
Presented By: Chairwoman Dawnafe Whitesinger
 
c. Presentation to Mogollon High School Division IV State Wrestling Champions 150 lb. Cael
Porter, 165 lb. Malaki Porter and 190 lb. Fisher Porter
 
Presented By: Supervisor Daryl Seymore
 
d. Recognition of Snowflake Division III Wrestling State Champion 132 lb. Terrell Kinlicheenie  
Presented By: Supervisor Jason Whiting
 
4. NAVAJO COUNTY JAIL DISTRICT: Board of Directors Session:  
 
a. REGULAR AGENDA:  
 
1. Consideration of approval to Direct Select under the Emergency Procurement procedures to
contract with Wexford Health Sources, Inc. for Inmate Health Care Services.
 
Presented By: Commander Ernie Garcia
 
5. NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT: Board of Directors
Session: 
 
 
a. REGULAR AGENDA:  
 
1. PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and approval of the amended Ordinance, Ordinance
Number 01-19 to clarify actions that are or are not permissible regarding outdoor fires,
correcting language for clarification purposes, grammar and spelling errors.  
 
Presented By: Catrina Jenkins
 
6. PUBLIC WORKS:  
 
a. Consideration of the installation of "No Parking" signs in the Mogollon Airpark.  
Presented By: Bill Bess
 
b. Consideration of Intergovernmental Agreement between Navajo County and Coconino County
for shared building plan review and inspection services.
 
Presented By: Bill Bess
 
c. Consideration of award of Contract #B22-01-010 Supplying and Delivering Liquid Asphalt
Products to Hawker & Evan Asphalt Co., Inc., and Cactus Transport, Inc.
 
Presented By: John Osgood

<!-- page 4 -->
d. PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and adoption of Resolution Number 06 - 2022
approving/denying a Master Development Plan amendment request to the previously approved
Master Site Plan in 2006, also known as Woodland Village, for up to 52-lot manufactured
residential subdivision. 
 
Presented By: Berrin Nejad
 
e. NAVAJO COUNTY FLOOD CONTROL DISTRICT: Board of Directors Session:  
 
1. CONSENT AGENDA: The following item(s) are considered to be of a routine or
administrative in nature and will be approved as a group unless a member of the Board wishes
to discuss a particular item: 
 
 
a. Intergovernmental Agreement with the Town of Snowflake for their request for funding
assistance for the Snowflake High School and Fire Station flood control project in the amount
of $50,000
 
 
2. REGULAR AGENDA:  
 
a. Consideration of a contract with The WLB Group in the amount of $88,095 to
prepare engineering construction plans for replacing the existing (3) corrugated metal pipes
(CPM) with multiple precast Concrete Box Culverts (CBC) to increase the drainage flow
capacity under Bourdon Ranch Rd.
 
Presented By: Bill Bess
 
7. COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE: Report from County Manager, County Attorney and Board
members.
 
 
 
8. ADJOURN  
 
Dated: March 4, 2022 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
Posted: 2:30 p.m. By: MWB                         
Melissa W. Buckley - Clerk of the Board

<!-- page 414 -->
Board of Supervisors Regular 2. g.        
Meeting Date: 03/08/2022  
Title: REP PC appointments
Submitted By: Rayleen Richards, Elections Director
Department: Elections
Motion before the Board:
Appointment of Daniel Thomas Spencer, James Robert Tapscott, Sandra Christine Johnson, Cara
Ann Massa, and Donna Anne Shurwin as Republican Precinct Committeemen for their respective
precincts
Background:
Appointment valid through Oct. 1, 2022. 16-822. Precinct committeemen; eligibility; vacancy;
duties; term B.  If the number of persons who file nominating petitions for an election to fill
precinct committeeman positions is less than or equal to the number of precinct committeeman
positions, the county board of supervisors may cancel the election for those positions not sooner
than seventy-five days before the election and appoint the person who filed the nominating
petition to fill the position. If no person has filed a nominating petition to fill a position, the
position is deemed vacant and shall be filled as otherwise provided by law. A precinct
committeeman who is appointed pursuant to this subsection after filing a nominating petition shall
be deemed an elected precinct committeeman. F. The term of office of a precinct committeeman is
two years and begins on October 1 after the primary election at which the precinct committeeman
was a candidate and continues until October 1 after the following primary election at which a
precinct committeeman is elected.

Board of Supervisors Regular 2. j.        
Meeting Date: 03/08/2022  
Title: LWDB MOAs for Mobile Job Center Site Use (Community Partners)
Submitted By: Stephanie Ray, Workforce Executive Director
Department: Northeastern Az Innovative Workforce
Motion before the Board:
Memoranda of Agreement between Northeastern Arizona Local Workforce Development Board
and Stanford Store, Old Concho Community Assistance Center, and Springerville-Eagar Chamber
of Commerce effective through June 30, 2024
Background:
 As part of the LWDB’s Strategic Initiative efforts, a pilot project is being implemented to
deploy a mobile job center in Apache County. The mobile job center will be utilized in
place of brick-and-mortar job centers and is intended to increase integrated service
delivery within the ARIZONA@WORK system and enhance partnerships with
community-based organizations to connect individuals throughout Apache County to a
broad array of resources.
The Mobile Job Center will deploy to a variety of sites throughout Apache County. The
initial phase of deployment will include provision of services at the following
community-based sites: Stanford Store, Old Concho Community Assistance Center, and
Springerville-Eagar Chamber of Commerce.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY22
Budgeted Y/N: N/A
Amount Requested: N/A
Fiscal Impact:
WIOA is federally funded
Attachments
MOA between LWDB and OCCAC 
MOA between LWDB and Springerville-Eagar Chamber 
MOA between LWDB and Stanford Store

<!-- page 445 -->
Board of Supervisors Regular 2. l.        
Meeting Date: 03/08/2022  
Title: MOU between LWDB and the Northeastern Arizona Community Resource Network
Submitted By: Stephanie Ray, Workforce Executive Director
Department: Northeastern Az Innovative Workforce
Motion before the Board:
Revised Memorandum of Understanding between The Northeastern Arizona Local Workforce
Development Board and the Northeastern Arizona Community Resource Network
Background:
The Northeastern Arizona Community Resource Network (NACRN) was formed as an Arizona
Nonprofit in May 2019. This MOU was originally entered into in 2019 and authorizes the
Executive Director of the Local Workforce Development Board (LWDB) to allocate time and
resources to building the capacity of NACRN and authorizes NACRN to research and develop
technology solutions, additional revenue streams, and relationship with key stakeholders that serve
job seekers. All of the duties NACRN is authorized to undertake are for the purpose of increasing
the capacity of the local workforce system and are allowable activities that can be conducted by
workforce boards under the Workforce Innovation and Opportunity Act. Because the LWDB is a
non-incorporated entity, the partnership with NACRN allows for the LWDB to conduct additional
activities through a legally incorporated, nonprofit entity. The Supervisors are considering this
MOU for approval on behalf of the Local Workforce Development Board. This constitutes a
minor revision to the original MOU with no material change to the terms or duration of the
agreement.

Board of Supervisors Regular 2. m.        
Meeting Date: 03/08/2022  
Title: IGA between Workforce Boards - Pinal County and Northeastern Arizona
Submitted By: Stephanie Ray, Workforce Executive Director
Department: Northeastern Az Innovative Workforce
Motion before the Board:
Intergovernmental Agreement between Pinal County Local Workforce Development Board and
Navajo County as Chief Elected Official for the Northeastern Arizona Local Workforce
Development Board
Background:
WIOA requires that Local Workforce Development Boards assess data on the Local Labor
Market and make strategic planning decisions based on that data. Historically, the data
accessible by the Northeastern Arizona Local Workforce Development Board has been
inadequate. To solve the problem, the workforce board has decided to purchase a
subscription service provided by EMSI, Burning Glass which will allow for better data
collection and reporting. The cost of the service is $25,000 per year which is a strain to the
existing budget. To mitigate that cost, the board has agreed to partner with Pinal County to
split the cost of the subscription. This IGA has been developed to detail the subscription
services and cost allocation.
Attachments
IGA between Workforce Boards - Pinal and Northeastern AZ

INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT BETWEEN  
PINAL COUNTY  
AND  
NAVAJO COUNTY AS CHIEF ELECTED OFFICIAL FOR  
THE NORTHEASTERN ARIZONA LOCAL WORKFORCE DEVELOPMENT BOARD 
FOR ONLINE ECONOMIC/WORKFORCE MODELING TOOL SUBSCRIPTION SERVICES 
This Intergovernmental Agreement (“Agreement”) is entered into pursuant to A.R.S. §§ 11-951 et seq. on this 
  day of        , 2022, by and between PINAL COUNTY, a political 
subdivision of the State of Arizona by and through the Pinal County Economic and Workforce Development Department 
(“Pinal”) and NAVAJO COUNTY, a political subdivision of the State of Arizona  (“Navajo”) for the provision and 
acquisition of online economic and workforce development modeling tool subscription services. Pinal and Navajo are 
sometimes referred to herein individually as a “Party” and collectively as the “Parties”. 
RECITALS 
WHEREAS, Pinal and Navajo may contract for services and enter into agreements with one another for joint 
or cooperative action pursuant to A.R.S. §§ 11-951 et seq.;  
WHEREAS, pursuant to by A.R.S. §§ 11-591 et seq. Pinal has established the Pinal County Economic and 
Workforce Development Department, which includes personnel qualified and appointed to perform the duties 
of the Department;  
WHEREAS, both Parties desire to efficiently share available resources in providing necessary tools to assist 
in the analyzing of each county’s economic and workforce landscape;  
WHEREAS, both parties agree to the value of timely and nationally recognized data sets  bring to the 
economic and workforce efforts of each; 
WHEREAS, the Parties to this agreement have determined it is cost effective to equally share subscription 
services and associated costs for online economic and workforce development subscription services;  
WHEREAS, Pinal and Navajo desire to enter into an agreement whereby the cost and available licesnses for 
access will be equally shared by both parties; 
NOW THEREFORE, Pinal and Navajo, pursuant to the above, and in consideration of the matters and things 
hereinafter set forth, do mutually agree as follows: 
AGREEMENT 
1. PURPOSE AND INTENT. The purpose of this Agreement is to set forth the financial and access parameters 
of shared subscription services for equal access to the use of  an agreed upon onlne economic and workforce 
development modeling tool between Pinal to Navajo Counties, and to address legal and administrative matters 
among the parties. 
2. TERM, TERMINATION, AND RENEWAL 
A. Unless terminated as otherwise provided in the Agreement, this Agreement shall become effective on  
the    day of      , 20         and shall remain in effect for  one (1) year 
thereafter unless otherwise terminated or renewed as provided herein. 
B. Either Party may terminate this Agreement by providing sixty (60) days advance written notice of 
termination to the other Party. 
C. Either Party to this Agreement may request a renewal of this Agreement, for subsequent one -year term(s) 
by providing sixty (60) days’ advance notice prior to the date of expiration of that Party’s intent to extend. 
Any renewal of this Agreement must be via a written mutually agreed upon and signed Amendment to this 
Agreement.

<!-- page 451 -->
3. MUTUAL OBLIGATIONS 
A. Under this Agreement Pinal County agrees to: 
1. An equal share of an agreed upon subscription to an agreed upon online economic and workforce 
development modeling tool; 
2. Reimburse Navajo County based on an agreed upon payment schedule for exactly one -half of the 
subscription cost for equal access to establish online accounts with the agreed upon tool; 
3. Provide a billing/invoice contact to Navajo County for any necessary cor respondence pursuant to this 
agreement; 
B. Under this Agreement Navajo County agrees to: 
1. Serve as the fiscal agent and administrator of the subscription; 
2. Recognize Pinal County as an equal “owner” of such subscription; 
3. Invoice Pinal County based on an agreed upon payment schedule for exactly one-half of the subscription 
cost for equal access to establish online accounts with the agreed upon tool; 
4. Provide a billing/invoice contact to Pinal County for any necessary correspondence pursuant to this 
agreement; 
4. BILLING AND PAYMENT. Billing, invoicing and payment of costs arising out of this Agreement shall be 
administered according to the schedule provided in Exhibit “A”. 
5. SUPERVISION, EQUIPMENT AND MATERIALS. No employee, agent, or servant of a Party s hall be 
deemed to be an employee, agent or servant of the other Party.  Each Party will be solely and entirely responsible 
for its acts and the acts of its employees, agents, servants, subcontractors, and volunteers during the performance 
of this Agreement.  Pinal County shall have sole supervisory authority over Pinal County personnel, operations, 
services, property, facilities and materials; and Navajo  County shall have sole supervisory authority over Navajo 
County personnel, operations and property. 
6. OPEN COMMUNICATIONS. Pinal and Navajo Counties shall maintain open communications between each 
Party’s designated point of contact [“POC”] (listed in Paragraph 9.A below) to ensure the agreed upon 
performances are provided and maintained throughout the term o f this Agreement. Parties shall maintain open 
communication regarding needs arising out of the Agreement. 
7. INSURANCE. Each Party acknowledges and affirms that it has appropriate and adequate insurance coverage 
for its official operations, duties and activit ies, and that it will maintain such coverage, at its own expense, for 
the duration of this Agreement. 
8. INDEMNIFICATION 
A. To the maximum extent permitted by law, each Party (as “Indemnitor”) agrees to indemnify, defend and 
hold harmless the other Party, its officers, officials, agents, employees, or volunteers from and against any 
and all claims, losses, liability, costs or expenses (including reasonable attorney’s fees) (hereinafter 
collectively referred to as “Claims”) arising out of actions taken in perfor mance of this Agreement to the 
extent that such Claims are caused by the acts, omissions, negligence, misconduct, or other fault of the 
Indemnitor, its officers, officials, agents, employees, or volunteers. If a Claim or Claims by third parties 
becomes subject to this Section, the parties to this Agreement that are the subject of the Claim or Claims 
shall expeditiously meet to agree upon a common and mutual defense pursuant to Subsection (B) below, 
including proportionate liability and proportionate payment of litigation fees, expenses and damages. 
B. The Parties when involved in a Claim or Claims brought by a third -party have a common interest in a 
coordinated defense in any lawsuit. In the absence of a conflict and to the extent applicable, the Parties agree 
to have one lawyer jointly represent the defendants in the lawsuit. To the extent applicable, the Parties agree 
to abide by the Memorandum of Understanding Regarding Joint Defense (“MOU”) between the Arizona 
Counties Insurance Pool (“ACIP”) and the Arizona  Municipal Risk Retention Pool (“AMRRP”). If 
applicable, each Party acknowledges that it has received a copy of the MOU from either ACIP or AMRRP. 
C. The obligations under this Section shall survive the termination of this Agreement.

<!-- page 452 -->
9. MISCELLANEOUS 
 
A. Notices. All notices to the other Party required under this Agreement shall be in writing and sent to the 
following personnel: 
 
If to Navajo/Northeastern Arizona LWDB:  
  
 Stephanie Ray   Name 
 
 LWDB Executive Director   Title

 180 N. 9th Street   Address 
 
 Show Low, AZ 85901   
 City, State, ZIP 
 
If to Pinal: Name 
 
    Title  
 
    Department 
 
    Address 
 
    
 City, State, ZIP 
 
B. Authority to Execute. The individuals executing this Agreement on behalf of the Parties hereto represent 
that they have authority to execute this Agreement on behalf of such parties, and represent that upon 
execution, this Agreement shall be binding and no further action is or  shall be necessary to make the 
Agreement enforceable in its entirety 
C. Modification. This Agreement shall not be modified or extended except by a mutually signed written 
agreement. 
D. Relationship of the Parties. Each Party shall act in its individual capacity  and not as an agent, employee, 
partner, joint venturer, associate, or any other representative capacity of the other party.  Each Party shall be 
solely and entirely responsible for its acts or acts of its agents and employees during the performance of this 
Agreement.   This Agreement shall not be construed to imply authority to perform any tasks, or accept any 
responsibility, not expressly set forth herein.  This Agreement shall be strictly construed against  the  creation  
of  a duty  or  responsibility  unless  the  intention  to  do  so  is  clearly and unambiguously  set forth herein.  
Nothing contained in this Agreement confers any right to any person or entity not a party to this Agreement. 
E. Waiver. The failure of either Party to insist in any one or more instances on performance of any of the terms 
or conditions of this Agreement or to exercise any right or privilege contained herein shall not be considered 
as thereafter waiving such terms, conditions, rights or privileges, and they shall remain in ful l force and 
effect. 
F. Governing Law and Venue. To the maximum extent possible, terms and conditions of this Agreement shall 
be governed by and interpreted in accordance with the laws and regulations of the State of Arizona. Any 
action relating to this Agreement shall be brought in an Arizona court in Pinal County provided that nothing 
herein shall be interpreted as an express or implied waiver of either party’s applicable immunity(ies).

<!-- page 453 -->
G. Interparty Dispute Resolution. If a dispute between the Parties arises out of or relates to this Agreement, and 
if the dispute cannot be settled through negotiation within sixty (60) days, the Parties agree first to try in good 
faith to resolve the dispute by mediation before resorting to litigation. The Parties shall mutually agree upon a 
mediator. Each party agrees to bear its own costs of mediation, and to split  the mediator fee. If mediation 
fails, any claim or action arising out of this Agreement shall be brought in  the Navajo County Superior Court 
in Holbrook, Arizona. 
H. Non-assignment. This Agreement has been entered into based upon the personal reputation, expertise and 
qualifications of the Parties. Neither P arty shall assign its interest in this Agreement, in whole or in part, 
without the prior written consent of the other Party. Neither Party shall assign any monies due or to become 
due to it hereunder without the prior written consent of the other Party. 
I. Entire Agreement. This Agreement represents the entire agreement between the Parties and supersedes all 
prior negotiations, representations or agreements, either expressed or implied, written or oral. It is mutually 
understood and agreed that no alteration or variation of the terms and conditions of this Agreement shall be 
valid unless made in writing and signed by the Parties. 
J. Severability. If any part, term or provision of this Agreement shall be held illegal, unenforceable or in 
conflict with any law, the validity of the remaining portions and provisions hereof shall not be affected. 
K. Conflicts of Interest. The provisions of A.R.S. § 38-511 relating to cancellation of contracts due to conflicts 
of interest shall apply to this Agreement. 
L. Other Duties Imposed by Law. Nothing in this Agreement shall be construed as relieving the involved 
public agencies of any obligation or responsibility imposed on it by law. 
M. Compliance with Laws and Policies. The parties shall comply with all applicable federal, state and loca l 
laws, rules, regulations, standards and Executive Orders, without limitation to those designated within this  
Agreement.  
N. No Joint Venture. It is not intended by this Agreement to, and nothing contained in this Agreement shall, 
be construed to, create any  partnership, joint venture or employment relationship between the Parties or 
create any employer-employee relationship between the Parties’ employees.  
O. No Third Party Beneficiaries. Nothing in this Agreement is intended to create duties or obligations to or 
rights in third parties not Parties to this Agreement or affect the legal liability of either Party to the 
Agreement by imposing any standard of care with respect to the maintenance of public facilities different 
from the standard of care imposed by law. 
P. Headings. The section headings throughout this Agreement shall not be used in the construction or 
interpretation hereof as they have no substantive effect and are for convenience only. 
Q. Non-Appropriation. Notwithstanding any other provision in this Agreem ent, this Agreement may be 
terminated if for any reason either party does not appropriate sufficient monies for the purpose of  
R. Maintaining this Agreement.  In the event of such cancellation, the canceling party shall have no further 
obligation other than for payment for services rendered prior to cancellation. 
S. Uncontrollable Events.  No Party shall be considered to be in default in the performance of any obligations 
under this Agreement (other than obligations of a Party to pay costs and expenses) if failure of performance 
is due to an uncontrollable event.  The term “uncontrollable event” means any cause beyond the control of 
the Party affected, including b ut not limited to flood, earthquake, storm, fire, epidemic, war, riot, civil 
disturbance or disobedience, labor dispute, and action or non -action by or failure to obtain the necessary 
authorizations or approvals from any governmental agency or authority or  the electorate, labor or material 
shortage, sabotage and restraint by court order or public authority, that by exercise of due diligence and 
foresight the Party reasonably could not have been expected to avoid and that by exercise of due diligence 
it will be unable to overcome.  A Party that is rendered unable to fulfill any obligation by reason of an 
uncontrollable event shall exercise due diligence to remove such inability with all reasonable dispatch 
T. Counterparts.  This Agreement may be signed in counterparts, each of which shall be deemed an original, 
but all of which together shall constitute one and the same instrument.

Board of Supervisors Regular 2. n.        
Meeting Date: 03/08/2022  
Title: IGA with Town of Taylor for Papermill Road Improvements - District III
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: John Osgood, Public Works Director
Department: Public Works
Motion before the Board:
Intergovernmental Agreement with the Town of Taylor for their request for financial assistance
for the Papermill Road improvement project in the amount of $50,000
Background:
The Town of Taylor is requesting financial assistance with their Papermill Road improvement
project.  The purpose of this IGA is to authorize the County to partner financially, as the County
and the Town share a common interest in the viability of this road. Financial participation and
maintenance partnerships of this kind are consistent with the County’s Board-approved policy of
utilizing HURF funds to supplement HURF eligible roadway maintenance activities throughout
the County, including tribal lands. 
For the Papermill Road project, the Town entered an IGA with ADOT to receive allocated
transportation dollars that come through NACOG through the HURF Exchange program. The
Town has received $771,500 from ADOT for the project which was to widen the road and extend
the turn lane that exists between SR77 and 700 West in the Town.The Town’s project
encompassed approximately 3,000 feet of widened asphalt as well as the installation of
much-needed storm drainage along the South side of the road. The Town shall be solely
responsible for the design, procurement, permitting and construction of the Project. The Town
understands and agrees that the County’s sole participation and obligation with respect to the
Project shall be to provide $50,000.
 
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY 2022
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: $50,000
Fiscal Impact:
The fiscal impact of the item is $50,000.  Funds have been budgeted in the Public Works CIP -
District III Special Road Project funds.

This Intergovernmental Agreement (“I GA”) is made by and between the Town of Taylor , an 
Arizona municipal corporation (“the Town”), and Navajo County, a political subdivision of the 
State of Arizona (“County”), pursuant to the parties’ authority under A.R.S §11-952. 
1. PURPOSE
The Town of Taylor is requesting financial assistance with their Papermill Road 
improvement project.  The purpose of this IG A is to authorize the County to partner 
financially, as the County and the Town share a common interest in the viability of this 
road. Financial participation and maintenance partnerships of this kind are consistent 
with the County’s Board approved policy of utilizing HURF funds to supplement HURF 
eligible roadway maintenance activities throughout the County, including tribal lands. 
This IGA supersedes any conflicting Agreements recorded between the same p arties. 
2. EFFECTIVE DATE AND TERM
This Agreement shall be effective on the date on which it is recorded by the Office of the 
Navajo County Recorder.  This Agreement shall remain in full force and in effect for a 
one (1) year period commencing on its effective date.   
3. TERMS, CONDITIONS, AND FINANCING
a. The Town entered an IGA with ADOT to receive allocated transportation dollars 
that come through NACOG through the HURF Exchange program. The Town has
received $771,500 from ADOT for the project which was to widen the road and extend 
the turn lane that exists between SR77 and 700 West in the Town.
b. The Town’s project encompassed approximately 3,000 feet of widened asphalt as 
well as the installation of much needed storm drainage along the South side of the 
road.
c. The Town shall be solely responsible for the design, procurement, permitting    
and construction of the Project. The Town understands and agrees that the 
County’s sole participation and obligation with respect to the Project shall be to 
provide $50,000. The Project is not a County project, and the  County is neither a 
partner nor a joint venturer with the Town in connection with the Project. The Town 
shall not represent to any consultant, contractor, or any other person that the 
County will have any responsibility or liability in connection with the Project except 
as set forth in this Agreement.
d. The County’s Board of Supervisorshas determined that it is an appropriate use 
of HURF funds to financially assist the Town with the Project and has bu dgeted 
$50,000 for this purpose, subject to the terms and conditions of this Agreement.
4. INDEMNIFICATION 
Each party shall be responsible for its own liability arising from the acts or omissions of 
its employees, or agents occurring in the course of performing this agreement. Neither 
party agrees to assume any liability of the other, or to defend, indemnify, o r hold 
INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT
between 
NAVAJO COUNTY 
and 
THE TOWN OF TAYLOR

<!-- page 459 -->
harmless the other party, against any claims of the other party, or liability of the other 
party, arising from the other party’s performance of the agreement. Instead, all rights to 
indemnify and contribution between the parties to this agreement shall be as provided 
by the law of the State of Arizona. 
5. HURF OBLIGATIONS
The Town understands and acknowledges that the funds used for the project 
described above are Arizona Highway User Revenue Fund (HURF) monies which are 
subject to audit by the Arizona Auditor General and assures Navajo County that the 
work will be performed on HURF qualified roads.  The Town agrees to fully 
cooperate in any audit of the use of these funds by the Arizona Auditor General. 
6. TERMINATION
a. Cancelation at Will
Either party may terminate this IGA upon written notice to the other party at least 
ninety (90) days before the effective date of termination. Notice to the County shall be 
addressed to the Navajo County Manager and notice to the Town shall be addressed 
to the Town of Taylor Town Manager.    
b. Agreed Cancelation This IGA may be canceled by mutual agreement.  
c. Violation of Agreement
Either party may terminate this IGA if a violation of any of the terms and conditions 
by the other party remains uncured thirty (30) days after the terminating party has 
delivered to the violating party a written notice specifying the nature of the violation. 
d. This Agreement is subject to cancellation pursuant to the provisions of A.R.S. 
§38511regarding Conflict of Interest. 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF , the authorized representatives of the parties have executed this 
agreement pursuant to resolutions duly adopted by their respective governing boards.

Title: Appointment Sadie Lister to the Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission -
Dist. II
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: John Osgood, Public Works Director
Department: Public Works Division: Planning & Zoning
Motion before the Board:
Appointment of Ms. Sadie Lister to the Planning and Zoning Commission representing District II
Background:
The Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission advises and makes
recommendations to the Board of Supervisors on matters of planning, zoning, and
subdivision platting.  Typical issues considered by the Commission include re-zoning
requests, Special Use Permits, and Zoning Ordinance amendments.  Currently, there is a
vacancy on the Commission in District II.  
For the District II vacancy, Ms. Sadie Lister has expressed strong interest in serving on the
Commission.  In her statement of interest, Ms. Lister states she will represent District II to
the best of her ability to further all initiatives of the Planning and Zoning Commission. 
Ms. Lister currently serves on the re-districting committee for District II, and has an
extensive background as a conservationist.  Further, she has served on numerous public
committees in support of her Indian Wells community.  Ms. Lister's appointment has been
reviewed and approved by Vice Chairman Peshlakai.

Title: Wendover Pines Subdivision - Time Extension Request, District IV
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Berrin Nejad, Community Development Director
Department: Public Works Division: Planning & Zoning
Motion before the Board:
Adoption of Resolution Number 05 - 2022 approving a 24-month time extension request to the
previously approved Master Site Plan, also known as Wendover Pines, for up to 450 residential
units, a commercial-retail space, and open space on approximately 295 acres
Background:
APN# 207-04-006A and 207-04-007B, T12N, R16E, S15 of the Gila and Salt River Meridian in
the Heber area. Owner: Wendover Pines, LLC. Agent: Solomon Sampson.
The applicant, Solomon Sampson, on behalf of Wendover Pines LLC., is requesting a 24-month
extension to the previously approved Master Site Plan for 450 residential units, a
commercial-retail space, and open space subdivision.  Due to the pandemic, progress was slowed
down, and the applicant is requesting a 24-month extension to fulfill the requirements to move
forward with the project.
The Wendover Pines project was approved for a Zone Change, from A-General to Special
Development, with Resolution # 08-20 and Master Development Plan with Resolution # 07-20 on
January 28, 2020.  The Master Development Plan approval had Condition #9 stating:
 
9.  Approval of the Master Development Site Plan is conditioned upon the submittal of the
Tentative Plat to the Planning and Zoning Commission, along with proof of compliance with
the above conditions, within 24 months of the date of Board of Supervisors Resolution.  If this
condition is not met, approval shall be revoked, and the Master Development Site Plan shall
become null and void.  This condition shall be conclusively deemed to have been satisfied
unless the Board of Supervisors causes a notice of revocation to be recorded in the Office of the
Navajo County Recorder within 30 days after the expiration of the 24-month period (or any
extension thereof).
The Planning and Zoning Commission heard this item in their January 2022 meeting and
recommended approval with a 4-0 vote.

ITEM #9 – MDP-A 22 -001 WOODLAND RIDGE MASTER DEVELOPMENT PLAN 
AMENDMENT: DISTRICT V: A request to amend a Master Development Plan approved 
in 2007, also known as the Woodland Villas development. The project is located at 534 N. 
Woodland Road, 9.6 acres in size, and located in Lakeside.  APN# 212-32-074, T9N, 
R22E, S26. Owner: Pinetop Lakeside Development, LLC. Agent: David Kutis and Daniela 
Dekova.  
 
Mrs. Nejad presented the item, noting the landmarks near the project located at 534 N. 
Woodland Road and stating that it was an amendment to the originally approved MDP 
back in January of 2007 , when the project was known as Woodland Villas.  The original 
MDP only allowed for site -built homes, they wished to amend this to allow for 
manufactured housing as well to help alleviate the community from costs, as well as 
change from the original 47 lot plan to 52 lots. 
 
The applicant , Daniela Dekova  and her representative David Kutis addressed the 
commission. They were asking for this change due to time constraints and wished to get 
their development up and running as quickly as possible. 
 
Vice Chairman Smith called for people to speak in favor of the project. 
 
Mark Coble spoke in favor of the project, noting his history with the Pinetop-Lakeside P&Z 
commission, citing that affordable housing is desperately needed in the community, and 
the current cost of housing is extravagant.  He felt that there was a strong need for this 
project and the changes made. 
 
Blaine Wilcock also spoke in favor of the project.  He also noted that there were some 
concerns for flooding in the bar ditches, expressing concerns over the impact the project 
would have on the drainage, and the responsibility of said drainage. 
 
Ferrell Fish spoke in favor of the project, but also had drainage concerns with the detention 
basin. 
 
Vice Chairman Smith called for comment for those against the project. 
 
Mary Ann Joseph spoke neither in favor nor against the project and presented her 
concerns via PowerPoint.  Her concerns mostly touched on drainage issues, the density, 
and involving outside agencies such as additional outside engineering and the Game and 
Fish department. 
 
Deborah Engerbretson, spoke against the project with concerns over drainage, 
Rick Joy spoke against the project voicing similar concerns, bringing up concerns for the 
wildlife- specifically the fish. 
 
Sharon Gilpin spoke against the project with concerns about traffic increase and asked if 
there were plans to widen the main road. 
 
Vice Chairman Smith  thanked everyone for their comments and reaffirmed Staff’s 
awareness of these issues, noting that they were aware of them back when the subdivision 
was first brought before them  and expressed confidence in their ability to address these 
issues in the future. Vice Chairman Smith  then asked County Engineer Bill Bess  to 
comment toward the engineering of the project; if it was still viable and if he could address

<!-- page 470 -->
Planning & Zoning Commission 1/20/2022 4/8 
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
some of the concerns the public had. Mr. Bess spoke to the drainage comments noting 
that the standards Navajo County uses for drainage reports and calculations have evolved 
and been refined over time and there is several different charts and rain data that can 
affect an outcome including the quality of the soil, all of which is put into a drainage report 
that they would review. 
Mr. Bess also addressed Mary Ann Joseph’s comments, specifically her noting the “20 -
foot benchmark” (grade difference). There is a difference between la nd with a flat slope 
versus a steep is the same amount of water was involved but the velocity is greater on the 
steeper slope. There are several other different factors that impacts run -off; driveways, 
pavement, sheds and roofs, and that both he and his Assistant County Engineer Will Flake 
are both licensed engineers with decades of experience doing these required drainage 
reports. He further noted that he did not see a need for additional outside review. 
 
Mr. Bess further noted that while the study was six years old, it is not a FEMA designation 
flood hazard area, but it may still be subject to flooding. Navajo County’s drainage and 
flood prevention ordinance identifies and covers these other areas that FEMA does not.  
 
The drainage and retention basins will be based off the County’s ordinance requirements. 
The Final Plat will have requirements for a detailed drainage study that will identify the 
needed size for the retention basin and drainage for the area, which will be reviewed by 
the County engineering staff.   
 
Mr. Bess further noted that the drainage issues Rainbow Lake Estates has today is 
because in 1960 when the plat was recorded the County didn’t have the drainage the 
regulations we have today. Rainbow Lake Estates did not receive the scrutiny that the 
requirements the Drainage Ordinance would have for new subdivisions. Navajo County’s 
current Drainage Code was adopted by the Board of Supervisors in the 90’s. 
 
There are requirements that the subdivision runoff cannot increase the predeveloped 100-
year-event into Rainbow Lake Estates. Detention Basins will be designed to detain the 
increased post development 100-year event. Staff will review and look at the amount of 
water coming out of the spillways and inspect the di scharge to measure and make sure 
that the run-off does not increase.   
 
As for fears of failures for the flood control measures, the design for the systems and basin 
will be reviewed by Navajo County , however the HOA for Woodland Ridge will be 
responsible for the maintenance of their drainage structures.   
 
Mr. Bess also noted Deborah  Engerbretson’s comments about density and stated that 
such matters were more a planning and development ordinance and would be reviewed 
by the planning department , however the  increase would also be added into the 
calculations for the hydrology report. 
 
Mr. Bess then stated that he had spent some time with both Mr. Rick Joy and Mrs. Mary 
Ann Joseph going over their concerns for Lee Reese Drive; they currently have contacted 
a surveying company to stake out and prepare to enlarge the drainage ditches to help 
reduce some of the issues they currently have with the water flow from the east to the 
west. Mr. Bess further noted that in order to contact Game and Fish they would have to 
add in additional roadways and areas into the study and spoke to Ms. Gilpin’s inquiry 
about increasing the size of Woodland Lake Road, saying that no, they did not have any 
plans to increase the size of the road at this time.

<!-- page 471 -->
Planning & Zoning Commission 1/20/2022 5/8 
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
Most of the concerns and requests that the public had toward this project tonight would be 
addressed further on in the process when the applicant submitted to move forward with a 
Final Plat. However, until he had a Final Plat submittal to review, there was not anything 
to report, good or bad. 
 
Vice Chairman Smith then asked the applicant to also address the issues. 
 
Mr. Kutis acknowledged flooding issues and hearing that drainage is the main concern  
brought before the commission, stated that their engineers have worked with the County 
over the past 6 months on just such issues; They found that with their design for drainage 
the historical runoff could be reduced from 24.9 cubic feet per second to 9.4 cubic feet per 
second, and were confident that the impact of their new drainage systems will be a benefit 
to the surrounding areas. 
 
Vice Chairman Smith turned it over to the Commission for comments, 
 
Commissioner Rogers asked asks if the concern for this item is the installation of mobile 
homes and not drainage. 
 
Vice Chairman Smith confirmed that while the drainage and flooding is a concern,  the 
discussion for this is premature as the expansion has not yet approved; she further noted 
that there were no objections to putting in manufactured homes and only discussion about 
flooding and drainage, she further pointed out how affordable housing is needed. 
 
Commissioner DeCross asked Mr. Bess about who designates the flood hazard areas, 
believing that the Army Corps of Engineers determine the flood areas. Mr. Bess confirmed 
but also said FEMA did some of the work as well, 
 
Commissioner DeCross asked Mr. Bess if this property was in a flood plain or a flood 
way, Mr. Bess replied that it was  not in a FEMA designated flood plain, it is su bject to 
some flooding but has not b een assessed due to the lack of a  study, the drainage not 
being enough to warrant a study. 
 
Commissioner Decross then noted that he remembered when they first brought this 
project before the commission but does not recall how many lots where in the subdivision, 
Mrs. Nejad replied that there was originally 47 lots, and Commissioner Decross had no 
more questions after confirming only 5 lots would be added, but he did make a follow up 
with a comment that he does not believe Game and Fish should be involved, as they would 
not really be able to address the issues and would only complicate matters. 
 
Mary Ann Joseph noted that she was receiving messages that the phone option for Zoom 
was not working, and some discuss ion was had about what to do. Vice Chairman Smith 
asked how the applicant wanted to proceed, and Mr. Kutis wanted to move forward with 
the process. 
 
Vice Chairman Smith wished to note that this meeting was merely a steppingstone in a 
long process, and that those who could not join the meeting would still be able to leave 
their comments with staff and be heard by the Board of Supervisors, as the final approval 
was under them.

Mary Ann Joseph interjected about setback requirements, Mrs. Nejad responded that the 
rezoning hearing portion of the process had already happened in the 2007 meetings, and 
it would not be changed. 
 
Commissioner Heisler asked why they needed to change the wording from Site -built 
only homes, and why they had made that a requirement, to which Vice Chairman Smith 
stated that the application had been received that way. 
 
Commissioner Decross wished to make a final note that again, the Planning and Zoning

recommend approval of the Master Development Plan  Amendment to the Board of

ITEM #10 – TP 22 -001 WOODLAND RIDGE SUBDIVISION TENTATIVE PLAT: 
DISTRICT V: A request for approval of a Tentative Plat to allow a 52 -lot manufactured 
home subdivision.  The site is zoned Special Development (SD), located at 534 N. 
Woodland Road, 9.6 acres in size, and located in Lakeside.  APN# 212-32-074, T9N,

Dekova. 
 
Mrs. Nejad then presented this item to the commission with a summary of the same site 
showing the layout and where the flood areas are. Mrs. Nejad noted that the applicant was 
a new owner and bringing this tentative plat forward as there was not a plat provided at 
the original approval. She presented a map to the commission, explaining where and how 
homes would be built along Duke Court in three phases. Berrin then mentioned who will 
potentially be providing services to new housing : Timber Mesa Fire district, electricity  
would be provided by Navopache Electric, natural gas by Unisource Energy services, solid 
waste by Larson Waste, sewage disposal by  Pinetop-Lakeside sanitation, tele phone, 
Internet, and cable by Sparklight. She then showed the commission the area of notification 
and where they had posted. All proper plat articles were filed and in compliance with the 
Zoning Ordinance . Staff received several phone calls about drainage. If approved 
submittal of the Final Plat should be within 24 months. 
 
The Applicant’s representative Mr. Kutis then spoke to the commission and said they were 
working diligently with Mr. Bess to ensure the draina ge isn’t an issue.  He said that the 
amount of refuse in the current drainage would filter directly into the lake, but their basins 
would be able to catch such refuse and make it easier to pick up the litter.  Mr. Kutis further 
stated that there would be an HOA in charge of maintaining the grounds and they would 
be sure to address the concerns brought up about the drainage with Engineering. 
 
Vice Chairman Smith called for comment from those in favor, and hearing none called 
for comment in opposition. 
 
Mary Ann Joseph spoke again to the commission with concerns about easement access, 
in her eyes there was not enough room for the utility companies and noted that the backing 
subdivision had homes encroaching into their own easements. She also wished to know 
what the setbacks were planned for the project.

Planning & Zoning Commission 1/20/2022 7/8 
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
The Vice Chairman asked for Staff to comment, Mr. Bess and Mr. Flake confirmed that 
the homes in the neighboring subdivision were encroaching into the  existing easements

Mr. Cooper stated that the setbacks would be 10 feet in the front, 15 feet in back, and that 
there would be a 10-foot water easement which would include the utility area, giving a 
designated 15-foot easement between the two of them, but also wished to note that all the 
finer details would be brought to the Board of Supervisors in the final plat, and that this 
was just a preliminary hearing. 
 
Vice Chairman Smith agreed that all the easements and issues will be discussed before

to assess the easement and confirm  that enough space is there as part of the final plat

Mr. Osgood asked Mr. Cooper for some clarification on the final plat process, as to whom

be presenting the final plat to the Board of Supervisors. 
 
Commissioner DeCross wished to view the map one final time as he had questions about

Commissioner DeCross also addressed M rs. Joseph’s comments on the homes that 
were currently encroaching into their utility easement - he remembered that there was a 
house inside that very subdivision that had been built encroaching into the utility

it had been standing since the 1960’s. He further stated that those homes that had

Mrs. Joseph asked why the encroachment hadn’t been caught before then, Mr. DeCross

STAFF REPORT  
PLANNING & ZONING 
 
Hearing Date: December 19, 2019 
Case No:  19-97 & 19-98 
Action:   Master Site Plan & Zone Change 
Applicant/Owner: Wendover Pines LLC 
Project Name: Wendover Pines Master Site Plan and Zone Change 
PARCEL INFORMATION  
 Address: No Address 
 
 APN:  207-04-006A & 207-04-007B 
 
 Legal Desc: Township 12 North, Range 16 East, Section 15 of the Gila and Salt River  
  Meridian in the Heber area. 
 
 District: IV 
 
 Area:  Heber, AZ 
 
 Parcel Size: Approximately 72.12 & 236.63 Acres, respectively 
 
STATED REASON FOR REQUEST:  
 
The applicant has requested approval of a Master Site Plan for up to 450 residential units, a 
commercial-retail space, and open space on APN 207-04-006A and 207-04-007B.  Additionally, they 
have requested a Zone Change on 207-04-007B from A-General to Special Development, which is 
contingent upon the aforementioned Zone Change. 
 
SURROUNDING USES:  
 
Direction Zoning/Usage 
North  U.S. Forest Service Land 
South SD, Single-Family Residential, Commercial, and Open Space 
East  U.S. Forest Service Land

<!-- page 476 -->
West U.S. Forest Service Land 
 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The subject property is located in the Heber area along State Route 260 and Forest Service Road 504. 
 
In the past, APN 207-04-006A has been approved for: 
 
- A 142 residential unit development with 6.35 acres of commercial through Board of 
Supervisors (B.O.S.) Resolution 89-84. 
- A Master Planned Commercial Shopping Complex through the B.O.S. Resolution 02-86. 
- The development of “High Country Apartments” through B.O.S. Resolution 32-87. 
- Four, twenty-four-unit apartments through B.O.S. Resolution 51-88. 
- A Zone Change to Special Development through B.O.S. Resolution 19-97. 
 
PROJECT DESCRIPTION: 
 
The proposed project area is in the Heber area along State Route 260 and Forest Service Road 504.  
The total proposed project area is for just under 309 acres, with a total of up to 450 residential units.  
This project is directly surrounded by U.S. Forest Service Land and is across from High Country 
Pines, a similar type of development with Special Development zoning. 
 
This proposal calls for a number of usages and densities.  The proposed usages on this property are as 
follows: 
 
Zoning District Total Units 
WP-7 (Single-Family, 7,000 Square Foot Minimum Lot Size) 260 
WP-10 (Single-Family, 10,000 Square Foot Minimum Lot 
Size) 
 
WP-20 (Single-Family, 20,000 Square Foot Minimum Lot 
Size) 
 
115 
WP-40 (Single-Family, 40,000 Square Foot Minimum Lot 
Size) 
 
53 
WP-80 (Single-Family, 80,000 Square Foot Minimum Lot 
Size) 
 
10 
WP-MF (Multi-Family, 6,000 Square Foot Minimum Lot Size) 
 
12 
WP-CR (Commercial-Residential) 
 
N/A 
 
The above zoning districts also have development standards and allowable uses, as required of Special 
Development.  The applicant’s zoning districts closely mirror Navajo County’s single-family, multi-
family, and commercial-residential districts as far as allowed uses.  The only land uses that are 
specifically excluded at this time are adult oriented businesses, medical marijuana dispensaries,

<!-- page 477 -->
medical marijuana dispensary offsite cultivation locations, and mobile homes. 
 
The applicant has indicated open space and amenities in order to accommodate for higher levels of 
densities within certain portions of the property.  The developer intends to have pathways along entry 
roads that connect to surface trails throughout the community and open space, with a central 
community park for the development containing amenities (ramada, fire pits, bbq grills, and outdoor 
game activities). 
 
In order to create a buffer with the National Forest, the applicant has indicated there will be a 30-foot 
setback/buffer from the National Forest area where perimeter walls, landscaping and backyards can 
exist, but no structures or walls over 8-feet in height. 
 
This development will have its own water system designed to service its residents.  The applicant is 
anticipating the need for an on-site well, booster pumps, and storage tank facilities.  The system will 
be designed per Arizona Department of Environmental Quality requirements.  The applicant intends to 
phase the water systems with the phasing of development for this project. 
 
In addition to a self-contained water system, this development will have its own sewer system.  Due to 
the topography of the site, the system will utilize a low-pressure force main with an individual grinder 
pump per home.  It is the intent of the developer to construct the wastewater treatment plant in phases 
as the phasing of Wendover Pines’ also occurs.  The proposed location for the wastewater treatment 
facility is on the eastern side of Forest Service Road 504, toward the northeast of the development. 
 
The developer has either organized or is in the final stages of organizing other utilities and services 
that would be necessary for a development, including: power, fiber, cable, and waste disposal. 
 
If approved, the applicant is hoping that this project will be phased based on market demand and 
purchases with initial phases.  As mentioned above, the applicant will be phasing infrastructural 
improvements to meet the needs of residents. 
 
ZONING DISTRICTS: 
 
ARTICLE 17 – SPECIAL DEVELOPMENT ZONE 
 
Section 1701 – Purpose.  
 
1. The purpose of the Special Development zone is to provide for the development of subdivisions 
and other land use projects containing various dwelling types, necessary commercial centers, off 
street parking areas, open space areas for recreation, and to preserve a rural atmosphere. 
 
2. Deviations from design standards for the specific zone or zones may be permitted provided open 
spaces are developed and maintained for the use of the residents and provided furt her that the 
parcel size, soil conditions, terrain, ground cover and other significant natural features favor this 
type of development. 
 
Section 1702 – Provisions Required Within the SD Zone 
 
Planned Unit Development segments are required within the Special Development Zone.  A Planned 
Unit Development or a Special Development project will provide for:

<!-- page 478 -->
1. Careful application of design which shall be encouraged in order that a more functional, 
aesthetically pleasing and harmonious living and working environment may be achieved within 
the County which otherwise may not be possible by strict adherence to the other articles of this 
ordinance.  In all cases a Planned Unit Development design shall together with dwelling types 
and land use, complement each other and harmonize with existing and proposed land uses in the 
area.  
 
COMPREHENSIVE PLAN/AREA PLAN:  
  
The proposed development is contained within the Heber/Overgaard Area Plan, and as such, both this 
Area Plan and Navajo County’s Comprehensive Plan must be consulted. 
 
Heber/Overgaard Area Plan 
 
Public Facilities and Services Element 
 
Issue: Utilities 
 
Goal: Provide utilities that are not currently available in order to help keep total utility costs low. 
 
 Objective 2: Develop sewer systems in the residential subdivisions so density of houses does 
 not create problems with septic tanks. 
 
- The developer is intending to create their own sewer system for this residential 
subdivision.  The wastewater treatment facility is indicated on the subject Master 
Development Site Plan.  In addition to explaining and showing plans for a wastewater 
treatment facility, the applicant has submitted a Preliminary Wastewater Master Plan. 
 
 Objective 3: Research alternatives to Arizona Water Co., to lower utility rates for water. 
 
- The is providing their own water system for their development, which will service the 
residents of the community. 
 
  Implementation Strategy 1: Require future developers of 6 or more dwellings per  
  parcel to provide sewer and sewage treatment facilities. 
 
o This proposed development is well above 6 dwellings and, as mentioned above, the 
applicant intends to provide sewer and sewage treatment facilities. 
 
Economic Element 
 
Issue: Tourism 
 
Goal: Benefit from our natural resources through the development of the tourism industry. 
 
 Objective 1: Establish recreational development. 
 
- This development is establishing recreational development internally through the inclusion 
of walking paths and trails as well as a centrally located park area containing a number of 
amenities.

<!-- page 479 -->
Objective 2: Establish recreational facilities. 
 
- See Objective 1 response above. 
 
 Objective 3: Establish recreational paths. 
 
- See Objective 1 response above. 
 
  Implementation Strategy 1: Encourage Navajo County to support and establish  
  recreational development in and around our area. 
 
  Implementation Strategy 2: Encourage private enterprise to support and establish  
  recreational development in and around our area. 
 
o The applicant is looking to support recreational uses through their inclusion 
internally.  This implementation strategy indicates that the Heber-Overgaard Area 
Plan supports this type of development. 
 
Land Use Element 
 
Issue: Residential 
 
Goal: Provide residential areas that are composed of quiet, safe neighborhoods that have easy access 
to schools, retail establishments, employment, and service establishments. 
 
 Objective 1: Minimize denuding of vegetation in new development areas – maintain rural 
 environment. 
 
- The narrative for this project has indicated the importance of the surrounding Ponderosa 
Pine Trees both to the development and the area.  Based on this importance, the developer 
has indicated that the development will be looking to maintain as much of the natural 
environment and vegetation as possible. 
 
Issue: Commercial 
 
Goal: Encourage retail, wholesale, service, and office uses with limited manufacturing capabilities. 
 
 Objective 1: Provide a vegetative buffer zone if a commercial area is to be located adjacent to 
 residential area. 
 
- The applicant will need to provide some form of a vegetative buffer between their 
commercial portion of their proposal and the residential portions of their project.   
 
 Objective 2: Neighborhood shopping centers or group commercial developments require 
 suitable locations with controlled access. 
 
- The commercial-residential portion of this development is located directly adjacent to State 
Route 260.  The location for commercial-residential is also a direct point of access for the 
entire development, making it a suitable, controlled point of access.

<!-- page 480 -->
Objective 5: Locate commercial areas for services and retail businesses convenient to 
 residential areas and locate on principal arterial, minor arterial, or collector roadways. 
 
- The commercial proposal with this community is located off of a major roadway, directly 
abuts the residential portions of this development, and is across from the already-
established High Country Pines subdivision. 
 
 Objective 6: Land area to encompass up to 5 acres in commercial developments. 
 
- The total proposal for the commercial portion of this development is 4.0 acres, which is 
under the 5-acre maximum suggestion made by the Area Plan. 
 
Issue: Parks/Greenbelts and Open Space 
 
Goal: Leave open space areas in a natural state for aesthetic and scenic purposes. 
 
 Objective 1: Maintain the rural and pioneer heritage of Heber/Overgaard by keeping essential 
 open space areas. 
 
- This development is maintaining essential open space areas internally and is including a 
30-foot buffer between the development and National Forest land where fencing, 
backyards, and smaller accessory structures can be built or maintained, but where nothing 
above 8 feet in height shall be constructed. 
 
Navajo County Comprehensive Plan 
 
4.0 Land Use Element 
 
4.7 Goals and Policies 
 
Goal 2: To administer the Land Use Element of the Navajo County Comprehensive Plan so as to 
further the purposes of the Plan. 
 
Policy 2-A: Approve zone changes that are consistent with the Land Use Element’s character 
areas, as shown on the Land Use Map.  However, the Land Use Element and Map shall not be 
used as the sole justification for making decisions on zone changes. 
 
- The proposed Zone Change portion of this request is within the Heber-Overgaard Area 
Plan.   
 
Goal 4: To provide equal opportunity for safe, decent, sanitary, and affordable housing for all 
residents of the County, regardless of race, color, religion, mental or physical disability, sex, 
sexual orientation, marital or family status, or national origin, in conformance with the Federal 
Fair Housing Act of 1988 and the Americans with Disabilities Act of 1990 and to provide a 
range of residential uses that offer housing opportunities for all County residents. 
 
Policy 4-D: Consider developing standards for development density that reflect the needs and 
goals of existing communities, whether incorporated or unincorporated, such as establishing 
medium to high density residential zoning for Community Villages and Rural edge areas and

<!-- page 481 -->
low to medium density for developing rural areas. 
 
- Heber-Overgaard is an established community, similar to the Community Village 
Character Area, but larger in size.  As a result, it would make sense that higher density 
developments be created in a hub of the County like this community. 
 
Goal 5: Improve the overall appearance of the County. 
 
Policy 5-A: Maintain natural scenic qualities of the County by identifying and protecting 
cultural resources; protecting wildlife habitat; natural plant communities and riparian areas; 
and encouraging protection of scenic vistas. 
 
- The applicant is currently in the process of working with Arizona Game and Fish 
Department to see if they have any recommendations for protecting plant and wildlife on-
site.  Additionally, the applicant is working to maintain as much of the natural vegetation, 
due to its value to the proposed community. 
 
Goal 6: Maintain compatible land use patterns while encouraging free market development. 
 
 Policy 6-A: Encourage the use of, and provide incentives (such as density bonuses) for, 
 implementation of community master plans. 
 
- The applicant has required some areas of higher density within the development.  To 
accommodate this higher level of density, the plan provides for a wide variety of 
residential uses, as well as open space and a commercial area. 
 
 Policy 6-B: Consider, if needed, providing for buffers between different land uses including, 
 but not limited to, setbacks, walls or fences, and landscaping. 
 
- The applicant is providing a buffer along the rear of their property that is adjacent to the 
National Forest.  Directly across the road is another Special Development Subdivision, 
from which this development would not then need to screen itself. 
 
 Policy 6-C: Require paved or other appropriately surfaced access to commercial and industrial 
 uses.  
 
- There is paved access to the commercial portion of this development via State Route 260 
and the developer intends to have paved roadways within the community as well. 
 
Goal 7: Facilitate commercial and industrial development, including business and technology 
uses, where appropriate to increase the sales tax and employment opportunities. 
 
 Policy 7-A: Encourage strategic placement of commercial development so as to attract and 
 locate shopping and employment uses that have paved or other appropriately surfaced access 
 and are easily accessible, particularly in or near established communities and cities, and in 
 communities with air and/or rail access. 
 
- The commercial portion of this proposal is located along State Route 260.  This is a 
strategic location within the properties itself.  Additionally, this location is within the 
community of Heber-Overgaard, making it strategic in relation to this already well-

<!-- page 482 -->
established community. 
 
Goal 8: Reduce the danger from fire for all residents living in a wildland urban interface  or 
near a National Forest boundary. 
 
 Policy 8-D: Continue to require multiple access routes into new large subdivisions and other 
 major developments for emergency purposes and to improve traffic circulation pursuant to the
 Subdivision Regulations & Requirements. 
 
- The Master Development Site Plan for this proposal shows three separate forms of access 
to this subdivision, which more than meets the requirements laid out. 
 
 Policy 8-E: Continue the review by the Fire Districts in the approval of subdivisions and 
 development projects with their respective jurisdictions. 
 
Goal 9: Actively plan to accommodate growth in Navajo County in an appropriate manner. 
 
Policy 9-B: Encourage higher density and intensity development in those areas of the County 
where services, including but not limited to water and sewer systems, paved rights-of-way, and 
schools, are available or can be created with new development. 
 
- This development is looking to develop its own water and sewage systems to be self-
sustaining in these respects.  Access leading into this development is paved.  The nearby 
community of Heber-Overgaard already has school systems in place. 
 
5.0 Circulation Element 
 
5.4 Goals and Policies 
 
Goal 1: Provide a range of circulation options that are safe and efficient and that complement 
local communities and the natural environment. 
 
 Policy 1-B: Coordinate land use and circulation planning to encourage comprehensive and 
 efficient development and growth patterns that support adjacent land uses, complement the 
 character of adjacent communities and neighborhoods, and mitigate impacts on the natural 
 environment. 
 
- The proposed use for this case is nearly identical to the residential use to the south, across 
State Route 260.  The applicant is creating buffers with the National Forest Land that 
mitigate impact on this development’s surroundings. 
 
Goal 2: Ensure quality design and development of the circulation system. 
 
 Policy 2-F: Require developers of private circulation infrastructure to meet minimum County 
 standards. 
 
- The developer of this project is intending to pave all internal roadways of their subdivision.  
They will be adding turn lanes and there will be improving and widening of State Route 
260.

<!-- page 483 -->
Goal 4: Provide for safe travel and access to property. 
 
 Policy 4-B: Continue working with developers and property owners to achieve safe and legal 
 access for all properties. 
 
- The proposed community has three points of access and the developer has been working 
with ADOT and the County to make sure that all points of access are both safe and legal in 
nature. 
 
 Policy 4-C: Require developers to install or pay for necessary circulation infrastructure 
 improvements to support access to and within major developments or subdivisions. 
 
- The developer will be providing their own paved roads within the subdivision.   
 
Policy 4-E: Include consideration of, and planning for, pedestrian, equestrian, handicapped-
accessibility and bicycle use when designing circulation infrastructure improvements through 
addition of trails, sidewalks, signage, pavement markings, or other treatments. 
 
- Internally, there will be walking paths and trails that move throughout the development for 
the use of residents. 
 
Policy 4-G: Approve subdivisions or rezonings only where existing street facilities are 
adequate or where the developer agrees to pay for off-site improvements necessitated by the 
proposed development.  Consider adoption of either a development fee program that would 
provide credits for such off-site improvements or another financing mechanism to reimburse a 
developer for a portion of its expenses when later development occurs that benefits from the 
improvements. 
 
- The applicant has been working with ADOT on Traffic Impact Studies and will be 
responsible for any off-site improvements that would be deemed necessary by this study, 
ADOT, and County Engineers. 
 
Goal 5: Improve non-motorized circulation networks and provide more opportunities for 
alternative modes of travel. 
 
 Policy 5-A: Encourage planning for non-motorized circulation infrastructure in new 
 development projects. 
 
- As mentioned above, the applicant is plans to incorporate walking paths and trails 
throughout this development. 
 
Policy 5-C: Encourage major developments and subdivisions to install pedestrian and bicycle 
connections to continue the development of a cohesive system of non-motorized circulation 
infrastructure when such facilities exist on adjacent developments or subdivisions. 
 
- See above. 
 
FINDINGS OF FACT: 
 
1. The item has been properly noticed and posted in compliance with Arizona Revised Statutes and

<!-- page 484 -->
Article 29. 
 
Postcards for this proposal were sent to all neighbors within 300 feet of the subject properties on the 3rd 
of December 2019.  A legal ad was run for this item in the Wednesday, December 4th, 2019 edition of The 
Holbrook Tribune notifying of the request.  On the same date, the property was posted with legal notices 
of the Planning and Zoning Commission Hearing. 
 
2. The change is in compliance with the Comprehensive Plan. 
 
The proposed Master Development Site Plan and Zone Change are in compliance both with the Heber -
Overgaard Area Plan and Navajo County Comprehensive Plan goals, policies, and actions 
(implementation strategies). 
 
3. The change will have minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general welfare 
of properties in the vicinity of the proposed use. 
 
As proposed, this development should have minimal adverse effects on the public health, safet y, and 
general welfare for nearby properties.

PLANNING AND ZONING RECOMMENDATIONS:  
 
Planning and Zoning Staff recommends that the Planning and Zoning Commission make the 
recommendation of approval of the Master Development Site Plan on APN 207-04-006A & 207-04-007B 
to the Navajo County Board of Supervisors, subject to the following conditions: 
 
1. All Federal, State, and Navajo County permits required for each lot must be obtained and adhered 
to. 
 
2. This Master Development Site Plan shall allow for a maximum of 450 residential units. 
 
3. A Traffic Impact Analysis (TIA), Master Drainage Plan, Sanitary Sewer Collection System Design 
Report, and Water Distribution Design Report for the development, prepared by a Professional

<!-- page 485 -->
Engineer, licensed in the State of Arizona, shall be submitted to and approved by the County 
Engineer’s office before the Tentative Plat is submitted to the Planning and Zoning Commission. 
 
4. All required offsite improvements identified in the TIA, Master Drainage Plan, Sanitary Sewer 
Collection System Design Report, and Water Distribution Design Report shall be constructed or 
financially assured before the stage of development is constructed that requires the improvement. 
 
5. Sufficient off-road drainage easements shall be made available to accommodate major drainage as 
required. 
 
6. Drainage structures, including detention or retention po nds, shall be proposed with the Final Plat, 
as required. 
 
7. All technical requirements of the Subdivision Regulations shall be met before approval of the 
Tentative Plat.  Variances shall be requested in accordance with Section 4.12. 
 
8. All technical requiremen ts of the Subdivision Regulations and Requirements, Flood Control 
Ordinance, Building Safety Ordinance, and applicable codes shall be adhered to and approved at 
the proper Tentative Plat, Final, or Building Permit and construction phase (to include, withou t 
limitation, the Traffic Impact Analysis, Flood Plain Study, Drainage Study, Construction 
Improvement Plans, and Grading Plans). 
 
9. Approval of the Master Development Site Plan is conditioned upon the submittal of the Tentative Plat 
to the Planning and Zoning Commission, along with proof of compliance with the above conditions, 
within 24 months of the date of Board of Supervisors Resolution.  If this condition is not met, approval 
shall be revoked, and the Master Development Site Plan shall become null a nd void.  This condition 
shall be conclusively deemed to have been satisfied unless the Board of Supervisors causes a notice of 
revocation to be recorded in the Office of the Navajo County Recorder within 30 days after the 
expiration of the 24-month period (or any extension thereof). 
 
Planning and Zoning Staff makes the recommendation that the Planning and Zoning Commission make the 
recommendation of approval of the Zone Change from A -General to Special Development on APN 207 -
04-007B to the Navajo County Board of Supervisors.
