INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT BETWEEN
Coconino County and Navajo County
for Shared Building Plan Review and Inspection Services
This Intergovernmental Agreement (“Agreement”) is made this 8 day of March 2022, by and 
between COCONINO COUNTY, a political subdivision of the State of Arizona, of 219 East Cherry 
Avenue Flagstaff, Arizona (“Coconino County”) and NAVAJO COUNTY, a political subdivision of 
the State of Arizona, 100 East Code Talkers Drive South Highway 77 P.O. Box 668, Holbrook, 
6025, Arizona (“Navajo County”) to authorize Coconino County’s Community Development 
Division and Navajo County’s Community Development Division to assist in Shared Building Plan 
Review and Inspection Services in partnership with one another.
WHEREAS, Coconino County is interested in entering into a shared services agreement 
with Navajo County to conduct building plan review and inspection services, on an as-needed 
basis, for development that occurs within the unincorporated areas of Navajo County; and
WHEREAS, Navajo County is interested in entering into a shared services agreement 
with Coconino County to conduct building plan review and inspection services, on an as-needed 
basis, for development that occurs in the unincorporated areas of Coconino County; and
WHEREAS, Navajo County and Coconino County are finding it difficult to recruit and 
retain qualified Building Plan Review and Inspection staff, due to retirement, turnover, and lack 
of qualified candidates; and  
WHEREAS, both counties think it necessary and vital that a Shared Service Agreement 
be implemented to be used during disasters or other extreme events that will overburden
individual departments; and
WHEREAS, both counties desire to participate jointly in activities that involve building 
plan review and inspection services based on a need by either entity due to: disaster, extreme 
work load experiences, lack of manpower experiences, or other.
NOW THEREFORE, pursuant to A.R.S. §11-952, authorizing contracts between public 
agencies for services or the joint exercise of powers common to both, and A.R.S. §11-863 which 
allows cities and counties to cooperate with each other for enforcement of such codes or rules 
and regulations adopted pursuant thereto to protect the health and welfare of its constituents, 
for and in consideration of the mutual obligations and covenants set forth herein, the parties 
agree as follows:
I. DURATION; TERMINATION
1. This Agreement shall be effective on the date first set forth above and shall continue in 
force and effect for a period of five (5) years and upon mutual consent of the parties.  
The Community Development Director for Navajo County and the Community

<!-- page 575 -->
Development Director for Coconino County may consent on behalf of the respective 
parties for extending this Agreement consistent with the language above.
2. This Agreement may be terminated by either Party upon thirty (30) days written notice 
of termination delivered to the other Party.  This Agreement does not contemplate
creation of any requirement of mandatory coverage for either Party because of 
manpower shortages or workloads.  It is agreed by both Parties that there must be 
adequate coverage in the jurisdiction called upon for resources.  If resources are not 
available to be shared at the time of the request by either Party, there is no obligation 
to provide such resources on the part of either Party.
II. SCOPE OF WORK
1. Navajo County Community Development Division and Coconino County Community 
Development Department provide Building Plan Review and Inspection Services to their 
respective constituents and use the International Code Council (ICC) Codes as their 
regulatory documents. Both counties intend to use the same versions of these 
documents.
2. Navajo County is experiencing a high volume of building permits for both commercial 
and residential projects and is burdened by staff shortages, which results in longer plan 
review turn-around times.  The County is also experiencing a high volume of residential 
projects and experiencing challenges during scheduled and unscheduled reductions in 
staffing levels.  
3. Coconino County is experiencing a high volume of building permits for both commercial and 
residential projects and is burdened by staff shortages, which results in longer plan review turn-
around times.  The County is also experiencing a high volume of residential projects, and 
experiences challenges during scheduled and unscheduled reductions in staffing levels.  
4. Coconino County and Navajo County wish to share common resources, such as Building 
Plan Review and Inspection Staff, to supplement their resources during staffing 
shortages resulting from vacancy, extended absences, and high-volume events.  This 
program shall serve as a cross-training opportunity and provide predictability and 
consistency for the customers working in both jurisdictions.
5. Coconino County will:
a. Provide Building Plan Review services for projects submitted to Navajo County
during manpower shortages due to conditions stated above.
b. Provide Building Inspection services for both commercial and residential projects 
during manpower shortages due to conditions stated above.
c. Provide other resources necessary for cross-training of staff and consistent 
application of the Codes.
d. Work collaboratively with Navajo County during future code adoptions.
e. Assist Navajo County with the implementation of similar County processes.
f. Communicate regularly regarding Program quality assurances.
g. Provide support during natural disasters or other emergencies that jointly affect 
both counties.
6. Navajo County will:

<!-- page 576 -->
a. Provide Building Plan Review services for projects submitted to Coconino County 
during manpower shortages due to conditions stated above.
b. Provide Building Inspection services for both commercial and residential projects 
during manpower shortages due to conditions stated above.
c. Provide other resources necessary for cross-training of staff and consistent 
application of the Codes.
d. Work collaboratively with the Coconino County during future code adoptions.
e. Assist Coconino County with the implementation of similar Navajo County
processes.
f. Communicate regularly regarding Program quality assurances.
g. Provide support during natural disasters or other emergencies that jointly affect 
both counties.
III. RECORD KEEPING
1. Both Parties agree to prepare, document and share all building plan review and 
inspection records that are applicable to each Party’s respective record keeping 
processes.
2. Both Parties agree to fully document any case that involves a job site conflict, stop work 
order, or other event that may result in a customer complaint and immediately notify 
the Building Official of the applicable jurisdiction.
3. The Building Official of the applicable jurisdiction shall issue all notices, 
correspondences, certificates of occupancy and other material that pertain to their 
jurisdiction.
IV. NO JOINT EMPLOYMENT
It is understood and agreed by both Parties that no employee of either Party shall be deemed 
to be an employee of the other Party due to this Agreement.  Moreover, this Agreement shall 
not be construed as creating any joint employment between either county.
V. CANCELLATION FOR CONFLICT OF INTEREST
Pursuant to A.R.S. § 38-511, either Party may cancel this Agreement without penalty or further 
obligation if any person significantly involved in initiating, negotiating, securing, drafting or 
creating this Agreement on behalf of a Party is, at any time while the Agreement or any 
extension of the Agreement is in effect, an employee or agent of  another Party of the 
Agreement in any capacity or as a consultant to the other Party of the Agreement with respect 
to the subject matter of this Agreement.
VII. INSURANCE
Both County’s shall each maintain sufficient liability insurance to cover each party’s respective 
activities associated with this Agreement.
VIII. INDEMNIFICATION

<!-- page 577 -->
Each Party (as “Indemnitor”) agrees to indemnify, defend and hold harmless the other Party (as 
“Indemnitee”) for, from and against any claims, losses, liability, costs or expenses, including 
reasonable attorney fees, (hereinafter collectively referred to as (“Claims”) arising out of this 
Agreement, but only to the extent that such Claims which result in vicarious or derivative 
liability to the Indemnitee are caused by act, omission, negligence, misconduct, or other fault of 
the Indemnitor, its officers, officials, agents, employees or volunteers.
IX. NOTICES
Any notice or other communication required or permitted to be given under this Agreement 
shall be in writing and sent to the address given below for the party to be notified, or to such 
other address, notice of which is given in accordance with this Section:
COCONINO COUNTY:
Community Development
Attn: Adam Hicks, CBO
2500 N. Fort Valley Rd. Bldg. 1
Flagstaff, AZ 86001
NAVAJO COUNTY:
Building Division
Attn: Robert Douglas, CBO
100 East Code Talkers Drive South Highway 77 
P.O. Box 668, Holbrook, Arizona 6025.
X. AUTHORITY TO CONTRACT
Each Party represents and warrants that it has full power and authority to enter into this 
Agreement and perform its obligations hereunder and has taken all required acts or actions 
necessary to authorize the same.
XI. GOVERNING LAW
This Agreement shall be governed, interpreted, and enforced in accordance with the laws of the 
State of Arizona.
XII. DISPUTE RESOLUTION
Either Party shall have the right to litigate any disputes which arise under this Agreement.  In 
the event any action at law or in equity is instituted between the Parties relating to this 
Agreement, the prevailing Party in the action will be entitled to its costs including reasonable 
attorneys’ fees and court costs from the non-prevailing Party.
XIII. INTEGRATION; MODIFICATION
Each Party acknowledges and agrees that it has not relied upon any statements, 
representations, agreements, or warranties, except as expressed in this Agreement, and that 
this Agreement constitutes the Parties' entire agreement with respect to the matters addressed 
in this document. All prior or contemporaneous agreements and understandings, oral or 
written, with respect to such matters are superseded and merged in this Agreement. This

<!-- page 578 -->
Agreement may be modified or amended only by written agreement signed by or for both 
Parties and recorded by the County Recorder, and any modification or amendment will become 
effective on the date so specified, but no earlier than the date of the recording by the County 
Recorder.
XIV. SEVERABILITY
If a court of competent jurisdiction shall hold any part or provision of this Agreement void or of 
no effect, the remaining provisions of this Agreement shall remain in full force and effect.
XV. WAIVER
No failure to enforce any condition or covenant of this Agreement shall imply or constitute a 
waiver of the right to insist upon performance of such condition or covenant, or of any other 
hereof, nor shall any waiver by either Party of any breach of any one or more conditions or 
covenants of this Agreement constitute a waiver of any succeeding or other breach of this 
Agreement.
XVI. HEADINGS
The headings used in this Agreement are for convenience only and are not intended to alter or 
affect the meaning of any provision of this Agreement.
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, the parties have caused this Agreement to be executed as of the day 
and year first above written.

Board of Supervisors Regular 6. c.        
Meeting Date: 03/08/2022  
Title: Supplying & Delivering Liquid Asphalt Products Contract #B22-01-010
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Eileen Smith, Account Specialist
Department: Public Works
Presented By: John Osgood
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of award of Contract #B22-01-010 Supplying and Delivering Liquid Asphalt
Products to Hawker & Evan Asphalt Co., Inc., and Cactus Transport, Inc.
Background:
This is an annual contract to provide the sale and delivery (as required), application, and other
aspects of liquid asphalt products used for the County's pavement preservation work in patching
potholes, chip seal application and fog seal treatments on public roads in Navajo County.
Sealed proposals were received by the Board of Supervisors at the Navajo County Governmental
Center, Holbrook, Arizona until 2:00 p.m. M.S.T., February 24, 2022, in strict accordance with
specifications on file in the office of the Navajo County Department of Public Works. No bids
were accepted after 2:00 p.m. M.S.T. The bids were publicly opened and read aloud at 2:15 p.m. 
Navajo County may award contracts to two bidders for each item, and select the bidder at the time
of purchase based on price, convenience, availability, and/or quality. The award of these contracts
provides the County the flexibility to utilize a second contractor should the lowest bidder not be
available. 
Staff recommends awarding contracts to Hawker & Evans Asphalt Co., Inc. and Cactus Transport,
Inc.; per the attached bid schedule.
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval.

Fiscal Year: 2022

Funds are budgeted in FY2022 Capital Improvement projects.

CONTRACT 
 
THIS CONTRACT is made and entered into as of  March 08, 2022 (the “Effective 
Date”) by and between Navajo County (“County”) and Cactus Transport, Inc.

Board of Supervisors Regular 6. d.        
Meeting Date: 03/08/2022  
Title: Woodland Ridge- Master Development Plan Amendment, District V
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Berrin Nejad, Community Development Director
Department: Public Works Division: Planning & Zoning
Presented By: Berrin Nejad
Motion before the Board:
PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and adoption of Resolution Number 06 - 2022
approving/denying a Master Development Plan amendment request to the previously approved
Master Site Plan in 2006, also known as Woodland Village, for up to 52-lot manufactured
residential subdivision. 
Background:
APN# 212-32-074, T9N, R22E, S26. Owner: Pinetop Lakeside, LLC. Agent: David Kutis and
Daniela Dekova. 
The applicants are requesting approval of a Master Development Plan for a 52-lot manufactured
subdivision, located at 534 N. Woodland Road on 9.6 acres in Lakeside. 
In December 2006, the Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission heard an item for the
subject parcel to change the zoning from C-R zoning to Special Development Zoning and to
approve a Master Development Site Plan for the project called Woodland Villas Subdivision.  The
proposal was for 47 residential sites with an average lot size of 4,400 square feet. In January 2007,
the item was approved by the BOS.  One of the stipulations of the approval was: 
Site Built Homes only.3.
The new proposal is for manufactured homes, therefore an amendment to the existing MDP is
needed.  The proposed site plan provides 52 lots, compared to 47 proposed in 2006.  The zoning
has been changed to SD- Special Development and is still within the residential designation;
therefore, staff would support the change from site-built homes to manufactured homes.
 
The applicants have been working with County staff to comply with the current codes and
ordinances for the changes. After several meetings and revisions, the staff is comfortable with
final submittals and recommends approval.
The Planning and Zoning Commission heard this item in their January 20, 2022 meeting via
zoom. There were two separate items scheduled for this project on the same night.  The first one
was the MDP-A (Master Development Plan Amendment) and the other was for Tentative Plat. 
There were neighbors attending the meeting with several concerns.  Mainly, they were concerned

<!-- page 594 -->
There were neighbors attending the meeting with several concerns.  Mainly, they were concerned
about engineering issues; specifically, flood issues from past incidents. 
The Master Development Plan amendment item concerned only the change from 47 site-built
houses to 52 manufactured homes. The engineering/flood issues concerned the Tentative Plat item
which was to be discussed later in the meeting.  Since the drainage and flooding issues were
brought up in the MDP-A item discussion, staff included a presentation submitted by one of the
neighbors, Mary Ann Joseph (see attachment) to be discussed during the MDP item.
The Planning and Zoning Commission made a motion to recommend approval of the MDP-A to
the Board of Supervisors 3-1 (McVicker opposing).  Once the final plat is complete, staff will
bring that forward for BOS approval. 
Recommendation: 
The Planning and Zoning Commission and staff recommend approval of Master Development
Plan amendment.

<!-- page 596 -->
PO Box 2250, Pinetop, AZ 85935 
 
MASTER DEVELOPMENT PLAN 
Name of Subdivision:  Woodland Ridge 
Developer:  Pinetop Lakeside Development LLC (“PLD”) 
PLD recognizes the immediate need for affordable housing in and around Pinetop‐Lakeside and 
the greater Show Low area.  We have secured the abandoned parcel of land located at 534 
Woodland Road to develop Woodland Ridge. 
Woodland Ridge will be a 52‐lot community designed to accommodate affordable 
manufactured homes that will offer our area the much‐needed housing families need.  It will 
include common area amenities for neighborly gatherings and other enhancements for the 
residents of the community. 
Nestled in the heart of Lakeside, Woodland Ridge is within walking distance to Blue Ridge High 
School, the WME movie theater, shopping and more. The location offers a very family‐friendly 
environment. 
The existing neighboring communities are currently zoned for manufactured homes, meaning 
that our community will blend in well with the existing neighborhoods. 
The community will have a full perimeter 6’ precast concrete wall, similar to existing 
subdivisions in and around Pinetop Lakeside. 
To keep with the surrounding area, there will be no street lights to maintain the serenity of the 
area. 
All sites will have mail delivery directly to their individual lots via personal mail boxes. 
The CC&R’s are being developed and an HOA will be formed to maintain the communities 
aesthetic values and clean all common areas. 
The roadway, will be deeded to the county for maintenance.

<!-- page 597 -->
PO Box 2250, Pinetop, AZ 85935 
 
The development will be constructed in three stages to minimize impact on surrounding 
neighbors. 
Stage 1 will include the construction of Duke Court along the north side of the community and 
include Lots 1‐15 and 46‐52. At the onset of Stage 1, the precast concrete wall will be 
constructed in it’s intirety. 
Stage 2 will include construction of Duke Court along the west side of the community and 
include Lots 16‐25 and 39‐45.  During this stage, the common area labeled Tract “c” on the 
Tentative plat will be developed as well. 
Stage 3 will complete Duke Court and the remainder of the lots.   
We have already seen a strong interest in this project and are accepting lot reservations with 
deposits going to Pioneer Title. 
We look forward to this project being approved and construction starting by early spring of 
2022. 
If you have any additional questions, please feel free to contact us at 928‐529‐0737. 
 
Pinetop Lakeside Development LLC 
(Developer)

ITEM #6 – CONSIDERATION OF APPROVAL OF MINUTES FROM THE COMMISION

Vice Chairman Smith called for a motion.  Commissioner DeCross made a motion to 
approve the minutes. Commissioner Murph seconded the motion.  Motion carried, 4 - 
0.  
 
ITEM #7 – SUP 21 -012 PINE ROSE MANUFACTURED HOME AND RV PARK, 
DISTRICT IV:   
A request for a Special Use Permit to build a 17-space manufactured home and RV park, 
including 14 manufactured home spaces and 3 RV spaces as well as a fourplex.  The 
property is  2.83-acres in size, located to the east of Porter Mountain Road, between 
Amanda Drive and Peterson Road, in the Lakeside area.  APN# 212-26-082N, T09N, 
R22E, S24 of the GSRM. Owner: Juan Rosales. Agent: Juan Rosales. (Continued from 
November 18, 2021 meeting) Item requested to be tabled to February meeting by the 
Applicant. 
 
Vice Chairman Smith called for a motion to table the item.  Commissioner Heisler 
made a motion to approve the Table. Commissioner DeCross seconded the motion.  
Motion carried, 4 - 0.  
 
 
ITEM #8 – TE 22-001 TIME EXTENTION FOR WENDOVER PINES: DISTRICT IV: A 24-
month time extension request to a previously approved Master Site Plan.  The approval 
was in January 2020 alongside a Zone Change for u p to 450 residential units, a 
commercial-retail space, and open space on approximately 295 acres.  APN# 207-04-
006A and 207-04-007B, T12N, R16E, S15 of the Gila and Salt River Meridian in the Heber 
area. Owner: Wendover Pines, LLC. Agent: Solomon Sampson. 
 
Mrs. Nejad presented the item to the commission. The item had been previously approved 
two years ago and as a condition they would have a 24 -month period to move forward 
with their project, however  the timeline was delayed because of the pandemic  and the 
applicant is requesting a 24-month time extension to continue making progress. She noted 
that Staff was in support of this, and recommended approval. 
 
The applicant Solomon Sampson addressed the commission and proceeded to explain 
that the pandemic made things difficult to continue in the timeframe given. 
 
Commissioner DeCross asked if this was the first extension given to this project, Mrs. 
Nejad confirmed that it was the first. 
 
Vice Chairman Smith called for a motion.  Commissioner DeCross made a motion to 
recommend approval of the Special Use Permit. Commissioner Murph seconded the 
motion.  Motion carried, 4 – 0

ITEM #9 – MDP-A 22 -001 WOODLAND RIDGE MASTER DEVELOPMENT PLAN 
AMENDMENT: DISTRICT V: A request to amend a Master Development Plan approved 
in 2007, also known as the Woodland Villas development. The project is located at 534 N. 
Woodland Road, 9.6 acres in size, and located in Lakeside.  APN# 212-32-074, T9N, 
R22E, S26. Owner: Pinetop Lakeside Development, LLC. Agent: David Kutis and Daniela 
Dekova.  
 
Mrs. Nejad presented the item, noting the landmarks near the project located at 534 N. 
Woodland Road and stating that it was an amendment to the originally approved MDP 
back in January of 2007 , when the project was known as Woodland Villas.  The original 
MDP only allowed for site -built homes, they wished to amend this to allow for 
manufactured housing as well to help alleviate the community from costs, as well as 
change from the original 47 lot plan to 52 lots. 
 
The applicant , Daniela Dekova  and her representative David Kutis addressed the 
commission. They were asking for this change due to time constraints and wished to get 
their development up and running as quickly as possible. 
 
Vice Chairman Smith called for people to speak in favor of the project. 
 
Mark Coble spoke in favor of the project, noting his history with the Pinetop-Lakeside P&Z 
commission, citing that affordable housing is desperately needed in the community, and 
the current cost of housing is extravagant.  He felt that there was a strong need for this 
project and the changes made. 
 
Blaine Wilcock also spoke in favor of the project.  He also noted that there were some 
concerns for flooding in the bar ditches, expressing concerns over the impact the project 
would have on the drainage, and the responsibility of said drainage. 
 
Ferrell Fish spoke in favor of the project, but also had drainage concerns with the detention 
basin. 
 
Vice Chairman Smith called for comment for those against the project. 
 
Mary Ann Joseph spoke neither in favor nor against the project and presented her 
concerns via PowerPoint.  Her concerns mostly touched on drainage issues, the density, 
and involving outside agencies such as additional outside engineering and the Game and 
Fish department. 
 
Deborah Engerbretson, spoke against the project with concerns over drainage, 
Rick Joy spoke against the project voicing similar concerns, bringing up concerns for the 
wildlife- specifically the fish. 
 
Sharon Gilpin spoke against the project with concerns about traffic increase and asked if 
there were plans to widen the main road. 
 
Vice Chairman Smith  thanked everyone for their comments and reaffirmed Staff’s 
awareness of these issues, noting that they were aware of them back when the subdivision 
was first brought before them  and expressed confidence in their ability to address these 
issues in the future. Vice Chairman Smith  then asked County Engineer Bill Bess  to 
comment toward the engineering of the project; if it was still viable and if he could address

<!-- page 615 -->
Planning & Zoning Commission 1/20/2022 4/8 
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
some of the concerns the public had. Mr. Bess spoke to the drainage comments noting 
that the standards Navajo County uses for drainage reports and calculations have evolved 
and been refined over time and there is several different charts and rain data that can 
affect an outcome including the quality of the soil, all of which is put into a drainage report 
that they would review. 
Mr. Bess also addressed Mary Ann Joseph’s comments, specifically her noting the “20 -
foot benchmark” (grade difference). There is a difference between la nd with a flat slope 
versus a steep is the same amount of water was involved but the velocity is greater on the 
steeper slope. There are several other different factors that impacts run -off; driveways, 
pavement, sheds and roofs, and that both he and his Assistant County Engineer Will Flake 
are both licensed engineers with decades of experience doing these required drainage 
reports. He further noted that he did not see a need for additional outside review. 
 
Mr. Bess further noted that while the study was six years old, it is not a FEMA designation 
flood hazard area, but it may still be subject to flooding. Navajo County’s drainage and 
flood prevention ordinance identifies and covers these other areas that FEMA does not.  
 
The drainage and retention basins will be based off the County’s ordinance requirements. 
The Final Plat will have requirements for a detailed drainage study that will identify the 
needed size for the retention basin and drainage for the area, which will be reviewed by 
the County engineering staff.   
 
Mr. Bess further noted that the drainage issues Rainbow Lake Estates has today is 
because in 1960 when the plat was recorded the County didn’t have the drainage the 
regulations we have today. Rainbow Lake Estates did not receive the scrutiny that the 
requirements the Drainage Ordinance would have for new subdivisions. Navajo County’s 
current Drainage Code was adopted by the Board of Supervisors in the 90’s. 
 
There are requirements that the subdivision runoff cannot increase the predeveloped 100-
year-event into Rainbow Lake Estates. Detention Basins will be designed to detain the 
increased post development 100-year event. Staff will review and look at the amount of 
water coming out of the spillways and inspect the di scharge to measure and make sure 
that the run-off does not increase.   
 
As for fears of failures for the flood control measures, the design for the systems and basin 
will be reviewed by Navajo County , however the HOA for Woodland Ridge will be 
responsible for the maintenance of their drainage structures.   
 
Mr. Bess also noted Deborah  Engerbretson’s comments about density and stated that 
such matters were more a planning and development ordinance and would be reviewed 
by the planning department , however the  increase would also be added into the 
calculations for the hydrology report. 
 
Mr. Bess then stated that he had spent some time with both Mr. Rick Joy and Mrs. Mary 
Ann Joseph going over their concerns for Lee Reese Drive; they currently have contacted 
a surveying company to stake out and prepare to enlarge the drainage ditches to help 
reduce some of the issues they currently have with the water flow from the east to the 
west. Mr. Bess further noted that in order to contact Game and Fish they would have to 
add in additional roadways and areas into the study and spoke to Ms. Gilpin’s inquiry 
about increasing the size of Woodland Lake Road, saying that no, they did not have any 
plans to increase the size of the road at this time.

<!-- page 616 -->
Planning & Zoning Commission 1/20/2022 5/8 
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
Most of the concerns and requests that the public had toward this project tonight would be 
addressed further on in the process when the applicant submitted to move forward with a 
Final Plat. However, until he had a Final Plat submittal to review, there was not anything 
to report, good or bad. 
 
Vice Chairman Smith then asked the applicant to also address the issues. 
 
Mr. Kutis acknowledged flooding issues and hearing that drainage is the main concern  
brought before the commission, stated that their engineers have worked with the County 
over the past 6 months on just such issues; They found that with their design for drainage 
the historical runoff could be reduced from 24.9 cubic feet per second to 9.4 cubic feet per 
second, and were confident that the impact of their new drainage systems will be a benefit 
to the surrounding areas. 
 
Vice Chairman Smith turned it over to the Commission for comments, 
 
Commissioner Rogers asked asks if the concern for this item is the installation of mobile

Vice Chairman Smith confirmed that while the drainage and flooding is a concern,  the

that there were no objections to putting in manufactured homes and only discussion about 
flooding and drainage, she further pointed out how affordable housing is needed. 
 
Commissioner DeCross asked Mr. Bess about who designates the flood hazard areas, 
believing that the Army Corps of Engineers determine the flood areas. Mr. Bess confirmed 
but also said FEMA did some of the work as well, 
 
Commissioner DeCross asked Mr. Bess if this property was in a flood plain or a flood 
way, Mr. Bess replied that it was  not in a FEMA designated flood plain, it is su bject to 
some flooding but has not b een assessed due to the lack of a  study, the drainage not 
being enough to warrant a study. 
 
Commissioner Decross then noted that he remembered when they first brought this

Mary Ann Joseph noted that she was receiving messages that the phone option for Zoom 
was not working, and some discuss ion was had about what to do. Vice Chairman Smith

Vice Chairman Smith wished to note that this meeting was merely a steppingstone in a

The Applicant’s representative Mr. Kutis then spoke to the commission and said they were

stated that there would be an HOA in charge of maintaining the grounds and they would

RESOLUTION NO.  XX-22 
 
 
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF 
SUPERVISORS APPROVING THE MASTER DEVELOPMENT 
PLAN AMENDMENT WOODLAND RIDGE MANUFACTURED 
HOME SUBDIVISION 
 
WHEREAS, an application for a Master Development Amendment was duly filed on 
December 21, 2021 by Pinetop Lakeside, LLC to construct a 52-unit subdivision; and 
 
WHEREAS, the application concerns the following real property in Navajo County: 
APN: 212-32-074, a portion of Township 9 North, Range 22 East, Sections 26 in the Lakeside 
area; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Master Development Plan Amendment application was considered by 
the Navajo County Planning & Zoning Commission at a duly noticed public hearing on January 
20, 2022; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors after considering the testimony 
and other evidence presented at the hearing, as well as the recommendations of staff, found that 
the Master Development Plan Amendment is consistent with the public health, safety and general 
welfare and should be approved subject to the conditions set forth herein; and 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that the Board of Supervisors hereby 
APPROVES the Master Development Plan Amendment per the Site Plan, subject to the following 
conditions:  
 
1. All Federal, State, and Navajo County permits required for each lot must be obtained and 
adhered to. 
 
2. This Master Development Site Plan shall allow for a maximum of 52 residential units. 
 
3. All technical requirements of the Subdivision Regulations and Re quirements, Flood 
Control Ordinance, Building Safety Ordinance, and applicable codes shall be adhered to 
and approved at the proper Tentative Plat, Final, or Building Permit and construction. 
 
4. Approval of the Master Development Site Plan is conditioned upo n the submittal of the 
Tentative Plat to the Planning and Zoning Commission, along with proof of compliance 
with the above conditions, within 24 months of the date of Board of Supervisors 
Resolution.  If this condition is not met, approval shall be revoked , and the Master 
Development Site Plan shall become null and void.  This condition shall be conclusively 
deemed to have been satisfied unless the Board of Supervisors causes a notice of revocation

<!-- page 625 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
to be recorded in the Office of the Navajo County Recorder within 30 days after the 
expiration of the 24-month period (or any extension thereof). 
 
 
 BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED  that the Secretary shall certify adoption of this 
Resolution and shall transmit certified copies to the applicant and the Clerk of the Board of 
Supervisors. 
 
APPROVED AND ADOPTED by the Board of Supervisors by a vote of __ ____ 
yeas and ______ nays on March 8, 2022.

Dawnafe Whitesinger, Chairwoman of the Board

HOME SUBDIVISION 
 
WHEREAS, an application for a Master Development Amendment was duly filed on 
December 21, 2021 by Pinetop Lakeside, LLC to construct a 52-unit subdivision; and 
 
WHEREAS, the application concerns the following real property in Navajo County: 
APN: 212-32-074, a portion of Township 9 North, Range 22 East, Sections 26 in the Lakeside 
area; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Master Development Plan amendment application was considered by 
the Navajo County Planning & Zoning Commission at a duly noticed public hearing on January 
20, 2022; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors after considering the testimony 
and other evidence presented at the hearing, as well as the recommendations of staff, found that 
the Master Development Plan Amendment is not consistent with the public health, safety a nd 
general welfare and should be denied; and 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that the Board of Supervisors hereby 
DENIES the Master Development Plan Amendment.  
 
 BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED  that the Secretary shall certify adoption of this 
Resolution and shall  transmit certified copies to the applicant and the Clerk of the Board of 
Supervisors.

Board of Supervisors Regular 6. e. 1. a.        
Meeting Date: 03/08/2022  
Title: IGA Town of Snowflake for Snowflake High School and Fire Station Flood Control
Project - District III
Submitted By: John Osgood, Public Works Director
Department: Public Works
Motion before the Board:
Intergovernmental Agreement with the Town of Snowflake for their request for funding assistance
for the Snowflake High School and Fire Station flood control project in the amount of $50,000
Background:
The Town of Snowflake received a Pre-Disaster Mitigation (PDM) Grant from FEMA for the
Snowflake High School and Fire Station flood control project and is requesting financial
assistance from the Navajo County Flood Control District to help with its non-federal share of
project costs.  The Town has undertaken and completed work on PDM flood control measures
which included installing a series of two detention basins and three street culvert crossings in the
area of Snow Flake High School and the fire station. 
The Town has requested financial assistance from the Navajo County Flood Control
District in an amount not to exceed $50,000.  Public Works has reviewed the request, finds
it consistent with the purpose of the flood control district, and supports the project. 
Funding is authorized in the Navajo County Flood Control District FY 2021-22 budget.  
 
 
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY 2022
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: $50,000
Fiscal Impact:
The fiscal impact of the item is $50,000.  Funds are available in the Public Works Flood Control
District budget.

1
INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT FOR
FLOOD CONTROL PROJECT FUNDING
THIS INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT is entered into as of March 8 , 2022
("Effective Date") by and between the Navajo County Flood Control District, a special taxing
district organized pursuant to Title 48, Chapter 21 of the Arizona Revised Statutes ("the
District"), and the Town of Snowflake, an Arizona municipal corporation ("the Town").
1. Premises.
1.1 The parties are authorized to enter into intergovernmental agreements for joint or 
cooperative action pursuant to A.RS. § 11-952.
1.2 The Town has received a Pre-Disaster Mitigation (PDM) Grant from FEMA for 
the Snowflake High School and Fire Station flood control project and is requesting 
financial assistance for its non-federal share of the project cost from the District.  
1.3 The Town has completed work to undertake PDM flood-control measures which 
included installing a series of two detention basins and three street culvert 
crossings in the areas of Snowflake High School and the fire station.
1.4 The District is authorized by A.R.S. § 48-3603(C)(3) to contract and join with a 
municipality such as the Town "in acquiring, constructing, maintaining and
operating flood control works, and regulating floodplains."
1.5 The Board of Directors of the District has determined that it is an appropriate use 
of District funds to financially assist the Town with the Project and has committed
$50,000 for this purpose, subject to the terms and conditions of thisAgreement.
2. The District's Obligations.
2.1 The District shall reimburse the Town for eligible costs of the Project, up to a 
maximum reimbursement of $50,000. Eligible costs are the actual expenses of 
design, engineering and other contractual services directly associated with the 
Project, property acquisition directly associated with the Project, and construction
and program management. The Town's administrative and other internal expenses 
are not eligible costs, nor are the expenses of improvements not directly related to 
flood control (such as landscaping, roads, or recreational facilities). The District
shall be solely responsible for determining whether an expense is an eligible cost.
2.1.1 It is understood and agreed by the District that the Town's share of eligible
costs may be paid from other funding sources which may be available to the
Town, such as federal or state grants. The availability of other funding
sources shall not reduce the District's share of eligible  costs as provided in
Section 2.1.

<!-- page 637 -->
2
2.1.2 Payment shall be made by the District within 30 days after receipt of each
reimbursement request. Reimbursement requests shall be in writing, shall
specify the costs in sufficient detail to enable the District to determine
whether they are eligible costs, and shall include the certification of the
Town Manager that the costs are eligible costs. Reimbursement requests
shall not be submitted more frequently than monthly. Reimbursement
requests shall only be made for completed work and will include copies of
original invoice documentation from the consultant and/or contractor to the
Town.
2.1.3 Throughout the Project, a reasonable balance shall be maintained between
the Town's contributions, which include the other funding sources as
described in Section 2.1.l, and the District's contributions. If the District
believes that a reasonable balance is not being maintained, it shall notify
the Town in writing and may suspend payment of reimbursement requests
until the issue has been resolved.
2.1.4 Upon reasonable notice of not less than 48 hours, the District or its
representatives may inspect and audit all books, papers and other records
pertaining to the Project and the Town's obligations under this
Agreement.
3. The Towns Obligations.
3.1 The Town shall be solely responsible for the design, procurement, permitting   and
construction of the Project. The Town understands and agrees that the District's sole
participation and obligation with respect to the Project shall be that of a funding 
source as described in Section 2.1. The Project is not a District project, and the

person that the District will have any responsibility or liability in connection with the
Project except as set forth in this Agreement.

ensure that all flood-control measures of the Project meet all federal, state and
local requirements.

undue delay or interruption.

maintenance, repairs and operating expenses. The Town shall maintain in force
comprehensive liability insurance adequate to insure against all risks associated with
the Project facilities.

Board of Supervisors Regular 6. e. 2. a.        
Meeting Date: 03/08/2022  
Title: Bourdon Ranch Road - Millet Swale Culverts - Dist III
Submitted For: Bill Bess, County Engineer/Assistant Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Bill Bess, County Engineer/Assistant Public Works Director
Department: Public Works
Presented By: Bill Bess
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of a contract with The WLB Group in the amount of $88,095 to
prepare engineering construction plans for replacing the existing (3) corrugated metal pipes
(CPM) with multiple precast Concrete Box Culverts (CBC) to increase the drainage flow capacity
under Bourdon Ranch Rd.
Background:
When the Millet Swale Dam breached in July, 2021 a large amount of stormwater runoff was
released into the Millet Swale existing channel.  When the flooding reached the (3) existing 48"
diameter Corrugated Metal Pipes (CMP) under Bourdon Ranch Road they were not large enough
to handle that large amount of stormwater runoff.  This caused the stormwater to back up and
flood the area adjacent to Bourdon Ranch Road across the entire width of Millet Swale
(approx.1800' wide).
The WLB Group will prepare engineering construction plans for replacing the existing the (3)
CMP's with multiple precast Concrete Box Culverts (CBC) to increase the drainage flow capacity
under Bourdon Ranch Rd, thus reducing the chance of flooding adjacent to the road.  The Precast
Concrete Box Culverts will greatly reduce the construction time and minimize the closing of
Bourdon Ranch Rd.
The WLB Group is an engineering consultant that is on our Professional Services provider list. 
They have offices in Flagstaff and Tucson which will provide sufficient staff expertise for this
project.
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval of this contract.
Attachments
Contract for Professioal Services

AGREEMENT made as of March 8, 2022, between Navajo County Flood Control 
District ("District") and The WLB Group, Inc. (“Consultant”). 
 
WHEREAS, District requires certain professional services for Bourdon Ranch Road / 
Millet Swale Culverts (“Project”); and 
 
WHEREAS, Consultant represents that it has the necessary expertise to provide such 
services in accordance with the terms of this Agreement. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the parties agree as follows: 
 
1. Services.  Consultant shall perform the work described in the Scope of Work dated 
February 23, 2022, and those additional items set forth in the Consultant’s schedule 
and fee proposal dated February 23, 2022, in a competent and professional manner to 
the satisfaction of District.  The Scope of Work and the Consultant’s schedule and fee 
proposal are attached hereto and by this reference incorporat ed herein.  If any 
incorporated term is inconsistent with the Agreement, this Agreement shall control. 
 
2. Consultant’s Expertise.  Consultant warrants that it has the ability, authority, 
capacity and professional expertise to perfo rm this Agreement.  Consultant shall 
provide an adequate staff of experienced  personnel, capable of and devoted to the 
successful accomplishment of the work to be performed hereunder.  Consultant shall 
assign specific individuals to key positio ns.  Once assigned to work under this 
Agreement, key personnel shall not be removed or replaced without District’s prior 
written approval.  
 
3. Independent Contractor.  Consultant acknowledges that  it is an independent 
contractor.  Neither party is or shall purport to be an agent, employee, partner, joint 
venturer or associate of the other.  An employee or agen t of one party shall not be 
deemed an employer or agent  of the other party for any purpose.  Taxes or Social 
Security contributions will not be with held from any paymen t by District, and 
Consultant shall be solely responsible for such matters.

4. Subcontracts.  Consultant shall not enter into any subcontract with respect to any of 
the work to be performed hereunder without District’s prior written approval.  All 
subcontracts shall comply with applicable federal and state laws and regulations and 
shall impose on the subcontractor substantially the same obligations as are imposed 
on Consultant by this Agreement with respect to those matters covered by Sections 8, 
9, 10, 12, 15 and 18.  Consultant is  responsible for full performance of this 
Agreement regardless of whether subcontractors are used. 
 
5. Time for Completion.  Consultant’s services will be provided in a manner consistent 
with the degree of care and skill ordi narily exercised by me mbers of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances.  Consultant’s services 
shall be performed as expeditiously as is consistent with professional skill and care 
and the orderly progress of the Project, and in accord ance with an agreed upon 
schedule for the performance of the Services hereunder.  Time limits established by a 
schedule approved by District shall not, except for reasonable cause, be exceeded by 
District or Consultant.  The Schedule shall be adjusted, if necessary, as the Project 
proceeds.  The date for final performance shall be extended by the number of days 
that performance is delayed by governmental approval or review procedures or other 
causes beyond Consultant’s reasonable control, as jointly confirmed in writing by the 
parties’ representatives. 
 
6. Payment.  District shall pay to Consultant  a not-to-exceed sum of eighty eight 
thousand ninety five dollars ($88,095.00) as payment in full for all services rendered 
by Consultant pursuant to this Agreement.  Payments shall be made within 30 days of 
District’s receipt of Consul tant’s monthly invoices.  E ach invoice shall detail the 
work performed during the billing period. 
 
7. Defects in Work.  District may reject any work product that fails to meet customary 
professional standards or Project specifi cations.  Consultant agrees to promptly 
remedy all such deficiencies.  The parties shall make a good faith effort to resolve any

Re: Bourdon Ranch Road / Millet Swale Culverts 
WLB No.: TBD 
Dear Mr. Bess: 
Thank you for the opportunity to submit our proposal for professional engineering services to provide for new 
culverts under Bourdon Ranch Road 
Project Understanding:  The Millet Swale Reservoir  is a reginal flood control basin maintained by the Silver 
Creek Flood Protection District.  In July 2021 the Millet Swale Reservoir dam breeched sending additional 
runoff downstream to Bourdon Ranch Road exceeding the capacity of the existing culverts that are located 
under the road.  JE Fuller (JEF) is working with Navajo County and ADWR to determine the new discharges 
from the dam since it was breeched.  
WLB will be providing a topographic survey of the area for the hydraulic modeling and construction documents 
for new culverts under Bourdon Ranch Road. The Project Location map below shows the location of the 
reservoir, proposed culvert locations under Bourdon Ranch Road, and aerial mapping limits.  
 
Figure 1 – Project Location 
The goal is to upsize the existing culverts under Bourdon Ranch Road to contain the 25-year discharge from the 
reservoir, if possible, to reduce the potential for overtopping the road due the additional flow being released.

<!-- page 651 -->
William Bess 
Navajo County 
Bourdon Ranch Road / Millet Swale Culverts  
February 23, 2022 
  Page 2 of 5 
   
500 N Beaver Street • Flagstaff, Arizona 86001 • (928) 779-1500 • FAX (928) 779-1501  
BASIC SERVICES: 
 
Task 1 – Topographic Survey: 
WLB will establish control to 8 panel points for Co oper Aerial to provide a 1”=40’ scale map with a 1 ft CI 
collection of DTM, Contours. The limits for the mapping are shown on Figure 1 above.  Survey control will be set 
to tie into the survey data that is being prepared by Silver Creek Flood Protection District.   
 
WLB will provide additional surveying for the locat ions of the proposed culverts under Bourdon Ranch R oad to 
as built the existing culverts and verify matchup e levations for the roadway and upstream and downstre am 
grading limits. Utilities not identified in the aer ial mapping from Cooper Aerial will be located as w ell to 
incorporate into the base map for the construction documents.   
 
WLB will add record right-of-way and parcel informa tion for adjacent properties.  The survey will be s ent to JE 
Fuller to use in their analysis of the hydraulic modeling of the Millet swale. 
 
Deliverables: includes the following: 
1.  Aerial Mapping Control Points (8) 
2.  1” = 40’ Scale map with 1‘ contour interval (Cooper Aerial) 
3.  Matchup topography and utilities along Bourdon Ranch Road 
4.  Record Right-of-Way 
 
 
Task 2 – Concept plans: 
With the topographic survey from Task 1 WLB will pr epare a concept plan for the culverts to be placed under 
Bourdon Ranch Road. Based on the flooding that happened and the initial estimates for the discharges that can 
reach the road the County is looking to install a b ox culvert or other pre-cast structure that will re duce the 
closures on the road during construction.  
 
To start WLB will prepare a scoping plan to present  options for the culverts that fit the constraints of the road 
geometry and depth of the channel upstream and down stream and work with JEF to model the options. WLB 
and JEF will meet with the county to revise the options to determine which ones to proceed with.  
 
There is an existing communication line along the west side of Bordon Ranch Road based on the utility markers 
in the area. WLB will work with Adams Trenching to locate and pothole the utilities in the area of the  culvert 
crossings. This will be done while the initial culvert sizes are being determined. Once the depth of potential utility 
crossings are determined WLB will review the initia l culvert sizing to determine if any utilities need  to be 
relocated or if the sizing for the culverts needs to be adjusted to avoid conflict with them. 
 
WLB will than prepare the concept plan for the culverts that will include: 
• Cover and detail sheets 
• Plan and profile of the proposed culverts (3) 
• Preliminary Inlet and Outlet structures for each culvert location

<!-- page 652 -->

WLB will prepare an order of magnitude estimate of costs for the culverts to include in the concept. 
 
Deliverables: includes the following: 
1.  15% Scoping Plan 
2.  Coordination meeting with JEF and County 
3.  Potholing of Existing Utilities 
4.  30% Concept Plans and Estimate of Probable Construction Costs 
 
Task 3 – Construction Documents 
WLB will meet with the County to resolve comments o n the 30% Concept plans. WLB will prepare a final s et of 
construction documents for the culverts. The construction documents will include the following: 
• Cover/Note/Detail Sheets (3 Sheets) 
• Construction Control Plan (1 Sheet) 
• Culvert Plan and Profiles (3 Sheets) 
• Asphalt replacements plan (1 Sheet) 
• Inlet and Outlet details for each Culvert (Up to 3 Sheets) 
• Erosion Control Plan 
 
Specifications will be included on the plans. An en gineer’s estimate for the construction cost will be  provided 
with the first Submittal. 
 
WLB will address one set of comments for the constriction documents for approval to construct.  
 
Deliverables: includes the following: 
1.  Resolution of Concept Plan Comments 
2.  90% Plan submittal 
3.  100% Final Construction Document Submittal 
4.  Engineers Construction Cost Estimate 
 
Task 4 – Drainage Report : 
WLB will prepare a Drainage report for the culvert designs.  The report will follow Navajo County Drainage Policy 
requirements to present the design calculations for  the culvert sizing as well as the inlet and outlet  structures. 
The drainage report will be included with the 90% plan submittal and will include one set of revisions if necessary. 
Excludes mapping of the new floodplains upstream and downstream of the culverts.  
Deliverables: Drainage Report for the new culvert crossings to include with the TASK 3 CD submittal.

We will bill monthly for services provided. Additional services authorized by the client will be billed monthly.  
 
SCHEDULE:  A detailed schedule will be provide following the Notice to Proceed.   
The duration for the control and aerial mapping wil l be approximately 30 working days from the notice to 
proceed.  
 
Assumptions 
1.  Survey control will be set by WLB for the aerial mapping using county supplied control and benchmark 
from the Silver Creek Flood Protection District survey prepared by others.  
2.  A sealed topographic survey is excluded but can be provided as additional service. 
3.  Topographic survey will be delivered as an AutoCAD file.  Combining the files from other sources will 
be any additional service. 
4.  Boundary survey of the parcels along Bourdon Ranch Road are excluded. Existing property information 
will be shown based on available record information.   
5.  Legal descriptions for easements or right-of-way are excluded.  TCE limits will be shown on the plans 
6.  Roadway replacement will be limited to what is required for the installation of the new structure. 
7.  The Budget includes 2 trips to the site for field review and meetings with the County. All other 
meetings will be through virtual conferencing. 
8.  The drainage report will be for the design of the culverts only. Mapping of the floodplains upstream 
and downstream of the culverts will be by others. 
9.  The work JE Fuller is providing for the H&H modeling to the Navajo County is through a separate 
contract. WLB will coordinate the culvert designs and topographic mapping with them. 
10.  A traffic study is not required. 
11.  A geotechnical report is not required. County will provide Structural paving sections to be used for the 
construction documents 
12.  Navajo County will provide ROW maps of the area and survey control.
